<!-- assumption-1: body_md -->
\label{main_assumption}
    \mbox{}
    \begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
        \item\par
        \noindent The empirical spectral distribution $\mu_N=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^N \delta_{\lambda_i}$ converges weakly to a probability measure $\mu$.
        
        \item
        $\mu$ is compactly supported on an interval $I = [\mu_{\rm min},\mu_{\rm max}]$, and has a density with respect to Lebesgue measure which is positive on the interior of this interval.

        \item The minimal and maximal eigenvalues converge to $\mu_{\rm min}$ and $\mu_{\rm max}$ respectively, that is $\lambda_1 \to \mu_{\rm min}$ and $\lambda_N \to \mu_{\rm max}$ as $N\to\infty$.        
        
        \item The Stieltjes transform of $\mu$ at the right end-point of the support is finite, i.e.
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:def_smu}
            s_\mu \coloneqq S_\mu(\mu_{\rm max})<\infty,
        \end{equation}
        where
        \begin{equation}\label{eq:def_stieltjes_transform}
            S_\mu(\lambda)
            \coloneqq
            \int_I \frac{\mu(dx)}{\lambda-x},\quad\lambda\notin (\mu_{\rm min},\mu_{\rm max}).
        \end{equation}
        
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end assumption-1 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:main}
    Let $M_N$ be a sequence of $N\times N$ symmetric matrices such that \Cref{main_assumption} holds. Then for any $B>0$, it holds uniformly for any $0\leqslant \beta \leqslant B$ that as $N\to\infty$
    \begin{equation}
        F_N(\beta)
        =
        \sup_{
        q \in [0,1)
        :
        \;
        \beta(1-q)\leqslant\beta_c
        }
        f_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,q \mu_{\rm max},q)
        +o(1)
    \end{equation}
    and the supremum is attained at $q=q_*(\beta)$.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:main_with_ext_field}
     Let $M_N$ be a sequence of $N\times N$ symmetric matrices such that \Cref{main_assumption} holds. Let $U_N$ be a sequence of subspaces of $\mathbb{R}^N$ such that $\dim(U_N) = O(1)$, and let $f=f_N$ be a sequence of functions $f: B_N \to \mathbb{R}$ such that $f(\sigma) = f(P^{U_N}\sigma)$ for all $\sigma \in B_N$. Assume furthermore that $f$ has finite Lipschitz constant $c$, uniformly in $N$. Then for any $\beta>0$, as $N\to \infty$
    \begin{equation}    
            F^f_N(\beta)
            =
            \sup_{m \in B_N \colon\beta(1-|m|^2) \leqslant\beta_c} F^f_{{\rm TAP}}(m)
        +o(1)
    \end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:Legendre_trans_trivial_bounds}
     For any probability distribution $\mu$ with bounded support
    \begin{equation}
        -z\mu_{\rm max}\leqslant L^{\ast}_{\mu}(z)-\left(-\frac{1}{2}\log(2z)-\frac{1}{2}\right)\leqslant-z\mu_{\rm min}
        \qquad
        \forall z>0.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Letting $l_m(x)=\tfrac{1}{2}\log(x - m)$ we have $l_{\mu_{\rm max}}(x)\leqslant L_\mu(x) \leqslant l_{\mu_{\rm min}}(x)$ for $x \geqslant \mu_{\rm max}$. Thus $l^\ast_{\mu_{\rm max}}(z)\leqslant L^\ast_\mu(z) \leqslant l_{\mu_{\rm min}}^\ast(z)$. Lastly $l^\ast_m(z) = -m z - \frac12 \log(2z) - \frac12$.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:free_energy_trivial_bounds}
     For any probability distribution $\mu$ with bounded support and any $\beta > 0$
    \begin{equation}
        \beta\mu_{\rm min}
        \leqslant
        F_{\mu}(\beta)
        \leqslant
        \beta \mu_{\rm max}.
    \end{equation}
    In particular, $F_\mu(\beta)$ is continuous at $0$.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Follows directly from \Cref{lem:Legendre_trans_trivial_bounds} and \eqref{eq:F_def}.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:xstar_legendre}
    Let $\mu$ be a probability distribution with bounded support. Then for any $z>0$ there is a unique maximizer $x^*(z)$ of the supremum in \eqref{eq:LegendreLstar}, and it satisfies
    \begin{equation}
        \left(\frac{1}{2z}+\mu_{\rm min}\right)\vee \mu_{\rm max}
        \leqslant
        x^*(z)
        \leqslant
        \frac{1}{2z}+\mu_{\rm max}
        \qquad
        \forall z> 0.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
The function $x\mapsto L_{\mu}(x)-zx$ is strictly concave, which implies that there is a unique maximizer. It is also differentiable on $(\mu_{\rm max},\infty)$, with $\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\left\{L_{\mu}(x)-zx\right\} = \frac12 S_\mu(x)-z$. If $s_\mu < \infty$ this also holds at $x=\mu_{\rm max}$. If $s_\mu<\infty$ and $\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\left\{L_{\mu}(x)-zx\right\}|_{x=\mu_{\rm max}}\leqslant 0$, then $x^*(z) = \mu_{\rm max}$, so the upper bound holds trivially. The lower bound also holds since $z \geqslant \frac12 s_{\mu}\geqslant \tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{1}{\mu_{\rm max}-\mu_{\rm min}}$. On the other hand, if $s_\mu<\infty$ and $\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\left\{L_{\mu}(x)-zx\right\}|_{x=\mu_{\rm max}}>0 $, or if $s_\mu=\infty$, then $x^*(z)>\mu_{\rm max}$ and $\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\left\{L_{\mu}(x)-zx\right\}|_{x=x^*}=0$. The claim then follows since for $x > \mu_{\rm max}$
    \begin{equation}
        0
        =
        \frac{\partial}{\partial x}\left\{L_{\mu}(x)-zx\right\}\overset{\eqref{eq:def_log_potential}}{=}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mu_{\rm min}}^{\mu_{\rm max}}\frac{\mu(dy)}{x-y} -z \in \left[ \frac{1}{2(x-\mu_{\rm min})}-z, \frac{1}{2(x-\mu_{\rm max})}-z  \right].
    \end{equation}
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:legendre_diff}
    Let $\mu$ be a probability with bounded support. Then $L_{\mu}^*(z)$ is differentiable on $(0,\infty)$, and
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:legndre_diff}
        L_{\mu}^{*'}(z)
        =
         -x^*(z),
    \end{equation}
    where $x^*(z)$ is the  unique maximizer in \eqref{eq:LegendreLstar}.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
We have $L_{\mu}^*(z) = L_{\mu}(x^*(z)) - z x^*(z)$ for all $z>0$. Note that $x^*(z) = \mu_{\rm max}$ for all $z \geqslant \frac12 s_\mu$. Thus $L^*(z) = L_\mu(\mu_{\rm max}) - \mu_{\rm max} z$ for such $z$, which is clearly differentiable in $z$ and satisfies \eqref{eq:legndre_diff}. If on the other hand $z \in (0,s_\mu/2)$, then $x^*(z) > \mu_{\rm max}$, and $x^*(z)$ is the unique solution of $\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\big\{L_{\mu}(x)-zx\big\}(x^*)= 0$. The implicit function theorem then implies that $x^{*}(z)$ is differentiable. The chain rule then implies that $L_\mu^*(z)$ is differentiable for $z<s_\mu/2$, and that \eqref{eq:legndre_diff} holds for such $z$.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:FE_lipschitz}
    Let $\mu$ be a probability with bounded support. Then $F_{\mu}$ is $|\mu_{\rm min}|\vee |\mu_{\rm max}|$-Lipschitz.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
The function $F_{\mu}$ is continuous on $[0,\infty)$ by \Cref{cor:free_energy_trivial_bounds}. By the definition of \eqref{eq:F_def} and Lemma \ref{lem:legendre_diff} it is also differentiable on $(0,\infty)$, with
    \begin{equation}
        F_{\mu}'(z)
        =
        x^*(z) - \frac{1}{2z}.
    \end{equation}
    Thus Lemma \ref{lem:xstar_legendre} concludes the proof of the lemma.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:sec2_main}
Let $\mu$ satisfy Assumption \ref{main_assumption}, and let $\Lambda\geqslant1$
be such that $\textup{supp}(\mu)\cup \textup{supp}(\nu) \subset[-\Lambda,\Lambda]$. Define

\begin{equation}
    \Delta_N(\nu,\mu)
    :=\sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}
      +|\nu_{\max}-\mu_{\rm max}|+\frac1{\sqrt N}.
\end{equation}
Then the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item For every $B>0$, there exists $C=C(B,\Lambda)>0$
    such that
    \begin{equation}
        \widehat F_\nu(\beta)
        \leqslant F_\mu(\beta)+C\Delta_N(\nu,\mu)
        \qquad\text{for all }\beta\in[0,B].
    \end{equation}

    \item For every fixed $\beta\in(0,\beta_c)$, there exists
    $C=C(\mu,\beta,\Lambda)>0$ such that
    \begin{equation}
        \widehat F_\nu(\beta)
        \geqslant F_\mu(\beta)-C\Delta_N(\nu,\mu).
    \end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, if $\mu_N$ is the empirical spectral measure of
a sequence satisfying Assumption~1, then
\begin{equation}
    \sup_{\beta\in[0,\beta_c]}
    \bigl|\widehat F_{\mu_N}(\beta)-F_\mu(\beta)\bigr|
    \longrightarrow0.
\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:fe_upper_bound}
    For any $\beta\geqslant 0$, if Assumption \eqref{eq:assumption_sec3} holds, then
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:fe_upper_bound}
        \hat{F}_{\nu}(\beta)
        \leqslant
        F_{\mu}(\beta)
        + 
        C_\beta\left(
            \sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}
            +
            \abs{\nu_{\max} - \mu_{\rm max}}
            +
            \frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
        \right),
    \end{equation}
    where $C_\beta=128[1+\beta\Lambda]e^{8(1+\beta\Lambda)}$.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:control_error_limit_A}
    Let $\beta \geqslant 0$ and $A = e^{4(1+\beta \Lambda)}$. Then for all $N\geqslant 1$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:control_error_limit_A}
        {\mathbb{E}}\left[
            \exp\left(
                \beta
                N\sum_{i=1}^N
                \lambda_i\frac{X_i^2}{\norm{X}^2}
            \right)
            1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{G_N^c}
        \right]
        \leqslant
        8e^{-N}
        {\mathbb{E}}\left[
            \exp\left(
                \beta N
                \sum_{i=1}^N
                \lambda_i\frac{X_i^2}{\norm{X}^2}
            \right)
            1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{G_N}
        \right].
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Standard tail bounds for the chi-squared distribution (e. g. \cite[Theorems 1 and 2]{ghosh2021exponential}) yield
    \begin{equation*}
        {\mathbb{P}}(G_N^c) \leqslant e^{-N I(A)} + e^{-N I(A^{-1})},
    \end{equation*}
    where
    \begin{equation}
        I(x) = \frac{1}{2}(x - 1 - \log x).
    \end{equation}
    Note that $I(x) \geqslant I(x^{-1}) \geqslant \frac{1}{2}(\log x - 1)$ for $x\geqslant 1$, and so $I(A)\geqslant I(A^{-1}) \geqslant 1 + 2\beta\Lambda$. Therefore, ${\mathbb{P}}(G_N^c) \leqslant 2 e^{-N (1 + 2\beta\Lambda)}\leqslant 2 e^{-1} \leqslant \frac{3}{4}$, which implies that ${\mathbb{P}}(G_N) \geqslant \frac{1}{4}$ and so
    \begin{equation*}
        {\mathbb{P}}(G_N^c)
        \leqslant
        8 e^{ -N (1 + 2 \beta \Lambda)}
        {\mathbb{P}}(G_N).
    \end{equation*}
    Using this together with
    \begin{equation*}
        \abs*{\beta N \sum_{i=1}^N \lambda_i\frac{X_i^2}{\norm{X}^2}} \leqslant \beta N \Lambda\quad\text{a.s.},
    \end{equation*}
    we obtain \eqref{eq:control_error_limit_A}.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:discretisation}
    Let $\beta\geqslant 0$, $0<\epsilon<1$, $A>1$, $G_N=\{|X|^2 \in [NA^{-1},NA]\}$ and suppose Assumption \eqref{eq:assumption_sec3} holds. Also, set
    \begin{gather*}
        R_k^2=(1+\epsilon)^k,\quad k\in{\mathbb{Z}},\\
        G_{N,k} = \{\norm{X}^2 \in [NR_k^2, NR_{k+1}^2)\},\quad k\in{\mathbb{Z}},\\
        K_{\epsilon,A}
        =
        \left\{
            k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
            :
            |k|\leqslant 1 + \frac{\log A}{\log(1+\epsilon)}
        \right\}.
    \end{gather*}
    Then for $N\geqslant 1$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:discretisation}
            {\mathbb{E}}\left[
                    \exp\left(
                        \beta
                        N\sum_{i=1}^N
                        \lambda_i\frac{X_i^2}{\norm{X}^2}
                    \right)
                    1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{G_N}
            \right]
            \leqslant            
            e^{ \epsilon \beta N \Lambda }
            \sum_{k\in K_{\epsilon,A}}
            {\mathbb{E}}\left[
                \exp\left(
                    \beta
                    \sum_{i=1}^N
                    \lambda_i\frac{X_i^2}{R_k^2}
                \right)
                1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{G_{N,k}}
            \right].
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
We note that $G_N\subset \bigcup_{k\in K_{\epsilon,A}} G_{N,k}$. On each $G_{N,k}$, we have
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:bound_on_Gnk}
        \abs{\norm{X}^2 - NR_k^2}\leqslant NR_k^2\epsilon,
    \end{equation}
    and so
    \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:bound_on_BNk}
        \left|
            N
            \sum_{i=1}^N
            \lambda_i
            \frac{X_i^2}{|X|^2}
            -
            \sum_{i=1}^N
            \lambda_i
            \frac{X_i^2}{R_k^2}
        \right|
        \leqslant
        \epsilon N \Lambda.
    \end{equation}    
    Summing the corresponding bounds, we obtain the required inequality.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:discreteFE_UB}
    Let $\beta \geqslant 0$, $\epsilon\in (0,1)$, and let $G_{N,k}$, $R_k$ be as in \Cref{lem:discretisation}. Then for any $N\geqslant 1$ and $k\in {\mathbb{Z}}$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:lem_discreteFE}
        \mathbb{E}\left[
            \exp\left(
                \beta \sum_{i=1}^N \lambda_i\frac{X_i^2}{R_k^2}
            \right)
            1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{G_{N,k}}
        \right]
        \leqslant
        \inf_{\eta} \left[e^{ N \epsilon \abs{\eta} R_k^2 - N \eta R_k^2}
        \prod_{i=1}^N
        \frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{2\beta\lambda_i}{R_k^2}-2\eta}}\right],
    \end{equation}
    where the infimum is taken over all $\eta<\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{\beta\lambda_N}{R_k^2}$.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Using \eqref{eq:bound_on_Gnk}, we get
    \begin{equation}
        1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{G_{N,k}} \leqslant \exp\left( \eta (\norm{X}^2-NR_k^2) + N \epsilon |\eta| R_k^2 \right),
    \end{equation}
    and therefore
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:discrete_FE_UB_step1}
        \text{l.h.s. of }\eqref{eq:lem_discreteFE}
        \leqslant
        \exp\left(
            N \epsilon \abs{\eta} R_k^2
            -
            N\eta R_k^2
        \right)
        {\mathbb{E}}\left[
            \exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^N X_i^2 \left(\frac{\beta \lambda_i}{R_k^2}+\eta\right)\right)
        \right].
    \end{equation}
    Recall that $X_i$ are i.i.d. standard Gaussians and that for a standard Gaussian random variable $\xi$ we have ${\mathbb{E}}[\exp(\theta\xi^2)]=(1 - 2\theta)^{-1/2}$ for $\theta<1/2$. Using this with $\theta_i = \beta \lambda_i / R_k^2 + \eta < 1/2$ for each $i$ yields
    \begin{equation*}
        {\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^N X_i^2\left(\frac{\beta\lambda_i}{R_k^2}+\eta\right)\right)\right]
        =        
        \prod_{i=1}^N \frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{2\beta\lambda_i}{ R_k^2}-2\eta}}.
    \end{equation*}
    Taking the infimum over all possible values of $\eta$ completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:UB_putting_together}
    Let $\beta\geqslant0$, $C=\beta \Lambda$ and suppose Assumption \eqref{eq:assumption_sec3} holds. Then for any $N\geqslant1$, $A\geqslant e^{4(1+\beta\Lambda)} $ and $0<\epsilon<1$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:UB_putting_together}
        Z_N(\beta)
        \leqslant
        9 e^{N C \epsilon}
        \sum_{k\in K_{\epsilon,A}}
        \inf_{\eta < \frac{1}{2} - \frac{\beta \lambda_N}{R_k^2}}
        e^{N \epsilon (1 + \epsilon) |\eta| A - N \eta R_k^2}
        \prod_{i=1}^N
        \frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{2\beta\lambda_i}{R_k^2}-2\eta}}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
This follows by combining the above lemmas. First use \Cref{lem:control_error_limit_A} (and the trivial inequality $8e^{-N}\leqslant 8$), then \Cref{lem:discretisation} (whose error gives rise to the constant $C$), and finally \Cref{lem:discreteFE_UB} (recalling that $R_k^2 \leqslant A(1+\epsilon)$ for all $k\in K_{\epsilon,A}$).
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:change_of_var}
    Let $\beta>0$, $R>0$ and  $\eta<\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{\beta\lambda_N}{R^2}$. Then for all $N\geqslant1$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:log_simplification}
        \frac{1}{N}
        \log\left(
            e^{-N\eta  R^2}
            \prod_{i=1}^N
            \frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\frac{2\beta\lambda_i}{R^2}-2\eta}}
        \right)
        =
        \log R
        -
        \frac{R^2}{2}
        -
        \frac{1}{2}\log(2\beta)
        +
        \varphi_{\nu}(\gamma;\beta),
    \end{equation}
    where $\gamma=\tfrac{(1-2\eta)R^2}{2\beta} > \lambda_N$ and 
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:vf_mu_def}
        \varphi_{\nu}(\gamma;\beta)
        =
        \beta \gamma
        -        
        L_{\nu}(\gamma),
    \end{equation}
    where $L_{\nu}$ is defined in \eqref{eq:def_log_potential}.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Using the change of variables
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:change_of_var_eta_to_gamma}
        \eta = \frac{1}{2} - \frac{\beta \gamma}{R^2},
    \end{equation}
    we obtain that the product in \eqref{eq:log_simplification} equals
    \begin{equation}
        \left( \frac{R}{\sqrt{2\beta}} \right)^N
        \prod_{i=1}^N \frac{1}{\sqrt{\gamma - \lambda_i}}
        =
        \exp\left(
            N\left(
                \log R
                -
                \frac{1}{2}\log(2\beta)
                -
                L_{\nu}(\gamma)
            \right)
        \right).       
    \end{equation}
    Substituting $-\eta R^2 = -\frac{R^2}{2} + \beta \gamma$ in the exponent in \eqref{eq:log_simplification} completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:UB_putting_together_2}
    Suppose Assumption \eqref{eq:assumption_sec3} holds. Then, for any $\beta>0$, $A\geqslant e^{4(1+\beta\Lambda)}$ and $0<\epsilon<1$, $N\geqslant1$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:UB_putting_together_2}
        \hat{F}_{\nu}(\beta)
        \leqslant
        -\frac{1}{2}
        -
        \frac{1}{2}\log(2\beta)
        +
        \inf_{\gamma > \lambda_N}
        \left[
            \varphi_{\nu}(\gamma;\beta) + c_1 \abs{\gamma} \epsilon
        \right]
        +
        c_2 \epsilon
        +
        (N \epsilon)^{-1} c_3,
    \end{equation}
    where $c_1 = 4 A^2 \beta$, $c_2 = \beta\Lambda + A$ and $c_3 = 5 + 4 \log A$.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Using the change of variables \eqref{eq:change_of_var_eta_to_gamma} and \Cref{lem:change_of_var} shows that the sum \eqref{eq:UB_putting_together} is at most
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:sum_UB_1}
        \sum_{k\in K_{\epsilon,A}}
        \inf_{\gamma>\lambda_N}
        \exp\left(
            N\epsilon(1+\epsilon)A\left(\frac{1}{2}
            +
            \frac{\beta\abs{\gamma}}{R_k^2}
            \right)
            +
            N\left(
                \log R_k - \frac{R_k^2}{2}            
                -
                \frac{1}{2}\log(2\beta)
                +
                \varphi_{\nu}(\gamma;\beta)
            \right)
        \right).
    \end{equation}
    Note that $\log R - R^2/2 \leqslant -1/2$ for all $R>0$ (with equality iff $R=1$). Furthermore,
    \begin{equation*}
    R_k^{-2} = R_{-k}^2 \leqslant (1 + \epsilon) A \leqslant 2 A
    \end{equation*}
    for $k \in K_{\epsilon,A}$ (see Lemma \ref{lem:discretisation}). Thus, the sum \eqref{eq:sum_UB_1} is not greater than
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:sum_UB_2}
        |K_{\epsilon,A}|
        \exp\left(            
            N\left(
                -\frac{1}{2}
                -
                \frac{1}{2}\log(2\beta)
                +
                \inf_{\gamma > \lambda_N}
                \left[
                    \varphi_{\nu}(\gamma;\beta)
                    +
                    4 A^2 \beta |\gamma| \epsilon
                \right]
                +
                A \epsilon
            \right)
        \right).        
    \end{equation}
    Next, we note that
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{2}\epsilon\leqslant\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\leqslant\log(1+\epsilon)\leqslant\epsilon,
    \end{equation}
    and so
    \begin{equation}
        \abs{K_{\epsilon,A}} \leqslant 2\left(1 + \frac{\log A}{\log(1+\epsilon)}\right) + 1\leqslant 3 + \frac{4\log A}{\epsilon},
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}\log\abs{K_{\epsilon,A}}\leqslant\frac{1}{N} \left(2 + \frac{4\log A}{\epsilon}\right)\leqslant\frac{2 + 4\log A}{N\epsilon}.
    \end{equation}
    Also,
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}\log 9\leqslant\frac{3}{N\epsilon}.
    \end{equation}
    First bounding the sum in \eqref{eq:UB_putting_together} by \eqref{eq:sum_UB_2} and then applying $\frac{1}{N}\log$ to both sides of the resulting inequality yields \eqref{eq:UB_putting_together_2}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:phi_min_UB}
    For any, not necessarily discrete, probability measure $\nu$ with compact support $[\nu_{\min},\nu_{\max}]\subset (-\infty,a]$, it holds
    \begin{equation}
        \varphi_\nu'(
        a
        +
        (2\beta)^{-1} ; \beta)
        \geqslant
        0.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Remark that $\varphi_{\nu}'=\beta-L_{\nu}'$. Moreover, $2L_{\nu}'$ is the derivative of the log-potential, that is, the integral of $x\mapsto (t-x)^{-1}$ with respect to $\nu$. The evaluation at $t = a + (2\beta)^{-1}$ ensures that for all $x\leqslant\nu_{\max}$ we have $(a + (2\beta)^{-1}-x)^{-1}\leqslant 2\beta$, which implies the result.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:sum_convergence_fixedR}
    Let $\delta>0$. Then for any $N\geqslant 1$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:sum_convergence_fixedR}
        \abs*{
            \varphi_\nu^{(k)}(\gamma;\beta)
            -
            \varphi_\mu^{(k)}(\gamma;\beta)
        }
        \leqslant
        \frac{1}{2\delta^{k+1}}
        W_1(\nu,\mu)
        \quad
        \text{if}
        \quad
        k=0,1,
        \quad
        \gamma
        \geqslant \max(\lambda_N,\mu_{\rm max})
        +
        \delta.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
We notice that $\varphi_{\nu}^{(k)}(\gamma;\beta)-\varphi_\mu^{(k)}(\gamma;\beta)$ is the difference of the integrals of $x\mapsto \tfrac{1}{2}\log^{(k)}(\gamma-x)$ with respect to the measures $\nu$ and $\mu$. Because both of these measures have bounded support, the Kantorovich--Rubinstein duality theorem states that for any $1$-Lipschitz function $f$ we have
    \begin{equation}
        \abs*{\int f(x)\nu(dx) - \int f(x)\mu(dx)} 
        \leqslant
        W_1(\nu,\mu).
    \end{equation}
    Since $\gamma\geqslant\lambda_N+\delta$ and $\gamma\geqslant\mu_{\rm max}+\delta$, the function $x\mapsto \log^{(k)}(\gamma-x)$ is $1/\delta^{k+1}$-Lipschitz on the union of the supports of $\nu$ and $\mu$, and so \eqref{eq:sum_convergence_fixedR} follows.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem:limit_range_gamma}
Let $\nu$ be a compactly supported probability measure, and let
$a\geqslant\nu_{\max}$. Then, for every $\beta>0$ and $\delta>0$,
\begin{equation}
    0\leqslant
    L_\nu^*(\beta)
    +\inf_{\gamma>a+\delta}\phi_\nu(\gamma;\beta)
    \leqslant \beta(a+\delta-\nu_{\max}).
\end{equation}
If additionally $\delta<(2\beta)^{-1}$, then the same bounds hold
with the infimum restricted to $a+\delta<\gamma\leqslant a+(2\beta)^{-1}$.
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Recall that
\begin{equation}
    L_\nu^*(\beta)
    =-\inf_{\gamma\geqslant\nu_{\max}}\phi_\nu(\gamma;\beta).
\end{equation}
Let $\gamma^*$ denote the minimizer of
$\phi_\nu(\cdot;\beta)$ on $[\nu_{\max},\infty)$.
Since
\begin{equation}
    \phi_\nu'(\gamma;\beta)
    =\beta-\frac12S_\nu(\gamma)
\end{equation}
is increasing, the function $\phi_\nu(\cdot;\beta)$ is
non-decreasing on $[\gamma^*,\infty)$.

If $\gamma^*\geqslant a+\delta$, then
\begin{equation}
    L_\nu^*(\beta)
    +\inf_{\gamma>a+\delta}\phi_\nu(\gamma;\beta)=0,
\end{equation}
Otherwise, $\gamma^*<a+\delta$, and
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
    L_\nu^*(\beta)
    +\inf_{\gamma>a+\delta}\phi_\nu(\gamma;\beta)
    &=\phi_\nu(a+\delta;\beta)-\phi_\nu(\gamma^*;\beta)\\
    &\leqslant\beta(a+\delta-\gamma^*)\\
    &\leqslant\beta(a+\delta-\nu_{\max}),
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
where we used that $\phi_\nu'(\gamma;\beta)\leqslant\beta$.
The lower bound follows directly by restricting the domain
of the infimum. Thus
\begin{equation}
    0\leqslant
    L_\nu^*(\beta)
    +\inf_{\gamma>a+\delta}\phi_\nu(\gamma;\beta)
    \leqslant\beta(a+\delta-\nu_{\max}).
\end{equation}

Finally, suppose that $\delta<(2\beta)^{-1}$.
By Lemma \ref{lem:phi_min_UB},
\begin{equation}
    \phi_\nu'\bigl(a+(2\beta)^{-1};\beta\bigr)\geqslant0.
\end{equation}
Since $\phi_\nu'(\cdot;\beta)$ is increasing,
$\phi_\nu(\cdot;\beta)$ is non-decreasing on
$[a+(2\beta)^{-1},\infty)$. Consequently,
\begin{equation}
    \inf_{\gamma>a+\delta} \phi_\nu(\gamma;\beta)
    =
    \inf_{a+\delta<\gamma\leqslant a+(2\beta)^{-1}}
        \phi_\nu(\gamma;\beta),
\end{equation}
which proves the assertion for the restricted infimum.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lem:inf_phi_N<=inf_phi}
    Let $\beta,\epsilon > 0$, and $0<\delta<(2\beta)^{-1}$. Then for any probability measures $\nu$ and $\mu$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:inf_phi_N<=inf_phi}
        \inf_{\gamma > \lambda_N}
        \left[
            \varphi_\nu(\gamma;\beta)
            +
            \epsilon |\gamma|
        \right]
        \leqslant
        -L^*_\mu(\beta)
        +
        c_4 \epsilon
        +
        c_5 \abs{\mu_{\rm max} - \lambda_N}
        +
        c_5 \delta
        +
        \frac{1}{\delta}W_1(\nu,\mu),
    \end{equation}
    where $c_4 =2\Lambda + (2\beta)^{-1}$ and $c_5 = \beta$.
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Let $a = \max\{\lambda_N,\mu_{\rm max}\}$. Consider the infimum in the l.h.s. of \eqref{eq:inf_phi_N<=inf_phi}.
    Shrinking the range of the infimum to $(a+\delta,a+(2\beta)^{-1})$, bounding $|\gamma|\leqslant |a|+(2\beta)^{-1} \leqslant c_4$, and using \Cref{lem:sum_convergence_fixedR} with $k=0$, we deduce that the l.h.s. of \eqref{eq:inf_phi_N<=inf_phi} is at most
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:using_sum_conv}
        \inf_{\gamma \in (a + \delta, a + (2\beta)^{-1}) }
        \varphi_\mu(\gamma;\beta)
        +
        c_4 \epsilon
        +
        \frac{1}{\delta}W_1(\nu,\mu).
    \end{equation}
    Bounding the infimum in \eqref{eq:using_sum_conv} using \Cref{lem:limit_range_gamma} then yields the claim.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
The proof is trivial for $\beta=0$ or $N=0$. Suppose $\beta>0$, $N>0$. By \Cref{lem:UB_putting_together_2} and \Cref{lem:inf_phi_N<=inf_phi} we obtain, for $0<\delta<(2\beta)^{-1}$,
    \begin{equation}
        \hat{F}_{\nu}(\beta)
        \leqslant
        F_{\mu}(\beta)
        +
        (c_2 + c_1 c_3) \epsilon
        +
        c_4 (N\epsilon)^{-1}
        +
        c_5 |\mu_{\rm max} - \lambda_N|
        +
        c_5 \delta 
        +
        \frac{1}{\delta}W_1(\nu,\mu),
    \end{equation}
    where $A= e^{4(1+\beta\Lambda)}$, $c_1 = 4 A^2 \beta$, $c_2 = \beta \Lambda + A$, $c_3 = 2\Lambda + (2\beta)^{-1}$, $c_4 = 5 + 4 \log A$ and $c_5 = \beta$. Remark that all the constant factors appearing in the above equation are upper bounded by
    \begin{equation}
        16(\beta\Lambda+1)(A+1)^2\leqslant 64[1+\beta\Lambda]e^{8(1+\beta\Lambda)}.
    \end{equation}
    
    If $\sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}<(2\beta)^{-1}$, then, setting $\delta = \sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}$ is possible and choosing $\epsilon=1/\sqrt{N}$ yields the claim with $C=128[1+\beta\Lambda]e^{8(1+\beta\Lambda)}$ (if $W_1(\nu,\mu)=0$, pick $\delta=1/N$).

    Now assume that $\sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}\geqslant (2\beta)^{-1}$. Then, also
    \begin{equation}
        \hat{F}_{\nu}(\beta)-F_{\mu}(\beta)\leqslant
        C\left(
        \sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}
        +
        \abs{\nu_{\max} - \mu_{\rm max}}
        +
        \frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
        \right),
    \end{equation}
    Indeed, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \hat{F}_{\nu}(\beta)\leqslant\frac{1}{N}\logE e^{N\beta\max\limits_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N} \abs{\lambda_i}}\leqslant\beta\Lambda,
    \end{equation}
    and by \Cref{cor:free_energy_trivial_bounds}
    \begin{equation}
        F_{\mu}(\beta)\geqslant -\beta\Lambda,
    \end{equation}
    so that
    \begin{equation}
        \hat{F}_{\nu}(\beta)-F_{\mu}(\beta)\leqslant2\beta\Lambda\leqslant 4\beta^2\Lambda\sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}.
    \end{equation}
    Furthermore,
    \begin{equation}
        4\beta^2\Lambda\sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}\leqslant 
        C\left(
        \sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}
        +
        \abs{\nu_{\max} - \mu_{\rm max}}
        +
        \frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
        \right),
    \end{equation}
    which proves the claim.

    Combining the two cases depending on the relation between $\sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}$ and $(2\beta)^{-1}$, we obtain the desired result.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lem:fe_lower_bound}
    Suppose that Assumption \eqref{eq:assumption_sec3} holds. Then, for any $\beta\in (0,\beta_c)$, there exits a constant $C>0$ such that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:fe_lower_bound}
        \hat{F}_{\nu}(\beta)\geqslant F_{\mu}(\beta)-C\left(\sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}+\abs{\lambda_N-\mu_{\rm max}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
For any $\beta \in (0, \beta_c)$, then there exists $\delta>0$ such that $\varphi_\mu'(\mu_{\rm max}+\delta;\beta)<0$.
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
For $\beta \in (0, \beta_c)$, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \varphi_\mu'(\mu_{\rm max};\beta)<\varphi_\mu'(\mu_{\rm max};\beta_c)=\beta_c-\frac{1}{2}S_\mu(\mu_{\rm max})=0.
    \end{equation}
    The statement now follows from the continuity of the function $\varphi_\mu'(\cdot;\beta)$.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lem:away_from_lambda_max}
For any $\beta\in (0,\beta_c)$, let $\delta>0$ be such that
    \begin{gather}
        \abs{\lambda_N-\mu_{\rm max}}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\delta,\\
        \varphi'_\mu(\mu_{\rm max} + 2\delta;\beta) \leqslant -\frac{2}{\delta^2}W_1(\nu,\mu).
    \end{gather}
    Then
    \begin{equation}
        \varphi'_\nu(\lambda_N + \delta;\beta)\leqslant 0.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
It follows from the assumptions that
    \begin{equation}
        \max(\lambda_N,\mu_{\rm max})
        +
        \frac{1}{2}\delta
        \leqslant
        \lambda_N
        + 
        \delta
        \leqslant
        \mu_{\rm max}
        + 
        2\delta.      
    \end{equation}
    Thus, by \Cref{lem:sum_convergence_fixedR}
    \begin{equation}
        \varphi'_\nu(\lambda_N + \delta;\beta)
        \leqslant
        \varphi'_\mu(\lambda_N + \delta;\beta)
        +
        \frac{2}{\delta^2}
        W_1(\nu,\mu)
        \leqslant
        \varphi'_\mu(\mu_{\rm max} + 2\delta;\beta)
        +
        \frac{2}{\delta^2}
        W_1(\nu,\mu)
        \leqslant
        0,
    \end{equation}
where we used that $\varphi'_\mu(\cdot;\beta)$ is increasing.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{lem:LB_cov_eta_star}
    For any $\beta\in (0,\beta_c)$, let $\delta>0$ be such that the conditions of \Cref{lem:away_from_lambda_max} are satisfied. Then the function $g_\nu$ has a unique minimizer $\eta^\ast<\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta\lambda_N}{R^2}$, which 
    satisfies
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:Q_moment_bounds}
        {\mathbb{Q}}_{\eta^\ast}[\norm{X}^2]
        =
        NR^2,
        \quad\quad
        \mathrm{Var}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{\eta^\ast}}[\norm{X}^2]
        \leqslant
        \frac{NR^4}{2\beta^2\delta^2},
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:eta_star_bounds}
        \frac{1}{2}
        -
        \frac{\beta\lambda_N}{R^2}
        -
        \frac{1}{2R^2}
        \leqslant
        \eta^\ast
        <
        \frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta\lambda_N}{R^2}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
We note that the function $g_\nu$ satisfies
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:phi_N_derivatives}
        g'_\nu(\eta)
        =
        {\mathbb{Q}}_\eta[\norm{X}^2] - NR^2,
        \qquad
        g''_\nu(\eta)
        =
        \mathrm{Var}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_\eta}[\norm{X}^2]\geqslant 0.
    \end{equation}
    and is convex. By \Cref{lem:change_of_var}, for all $\eta<\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta\lambda_N}{R^2}$ we have
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}g_\nu(\eta) =
            \log R
            -
            \frac{R^2}{2}
            -
            \frac{1}{2}\log(2\beta)
            +
            \varphi_\nu(\gamma;\beta),
    \end{equation}
    where
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:LB_change_of_var}
        \gamma = \gamma(\eta) = \frac{(1-2\eta)R^2}{2\beta},
    \end{equation}
    and so
    \begin{equation}
        g_\nu'(\eta) = -\frac{NR^2}{\beta} \varphi_\nu'(\gamma;\beta).
    \end{equation}
    Therefore, by \Cref{lem:away_from_lambda_max}, $g_\nu'(\eta_\delta)\geqslant 0$ for $\eta_\delta$ such that $\gamma(\eta_\delta)=\lambda_N+\delta$. Furthermore, $\varphi'_\nu(\cdot;\beta)$ is strictly increasing and $\varphi_\nu'(\gamma;\beta) \to \beta$ as $\gamma\to +\infty$, so $g_\nu'$ is strictly increasing and $ g_\nu'(\eta) \to -NR^2$ as $\eta \to -\infty$. Thus, $g_\nu$ has a unique minimizer $\eta^*\leqslant\eta_\delta$, and this minimizer satisfies $g_\nu'(\eta^*)=0$ and $\gamma^*=\gamma(\eta^*)\geqslant \gamma(\eta_\delta)=\lambda_N+\delta$.
    Therefore, \eqref{eq:phi_N_derivatives} implies that
    \begin{equation}
        {\mathbb{Q}}_{\eta_*}[\norm{X}^2]=NR^2.
    \end{equation}
    Furthermore,
    \begin{equation}
        0
        \leqslant
        \varphi_\nu''(\gamma;\beta)
        =
        -L_\nu''(\gamma)
        =
        \frac{1}{2N}\sum_{i=1}^N \frac{1}{(\gamma-\lambda_i)^2}
        \leqslant
        \frac{1}{2(\gamma-\lambda_N)^2},
    \end{equation}
    and so
    \begin{equation}
        0
        \leqslant
        g_\nu''(\eta^*)
        =
        \frac{NR^4}{\beta^2}
        \varphi_\nu''(\gamma^*;\beta)
        \leqslant
        \frac{NR^4}{2\beta^2\delta^2}.
    \end{equation}

    Lastly, using \Cref{lem:phi_min_UB}, we get
    \begin{equation}
        \eta^\ast=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta\gamma^*}{R^2}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta}{R^2}\left(\lambda_N+\frac{1}{2\beta}\right)=\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta\lambda_N}{R^2}\right)-\frac{1}{2R^2},
    \end{equation}
    which implies \eqref{eq:eta_star_bounds} and completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
\label{lem:g_lower_bound}
    For $\eta^\ast$ defined in \Cref{lem:LB_cov_eta_star} and $R=1$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:g_lower_bound}
        \frac{1}{N}g_\nu(\eta^\ast)\geqslant F_{\mu}(\beta)-(1+\beta)\sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}-\beta\abs{\lambda_N-\mu_{\rm max}}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
By the definition of $\eta^\ast$, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}g_\nu(\eta^\ast)=\inf_{\eta<\frac{1}{2}-\beta\lambda_N} \frac{1}{N}\log{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\left(\beta\sum_{i=1}^N \lambda_iX_i^2+\eta(\norm{X}^2-N)\right)\right].
    \end{equation}
    Changing the variable to $\gamma=\tfrac{1-2\eta}{2\beta}$, we get
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
        \frac{1}{N}g_\nu(\eta^\ast)=\inf_{\gamma>\lambda_N}
        \left\{
        -\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\log(2\beta)+\beta\gamma-\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{i=1}^N \log(\gamma-\lambda_i)
        \right\}.
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    
    Next, fix some $\delta>0$ and apply the result of Lemma \ref{lem:limit_range_gamma} with measure $\nu$, $(\mu_{\rm max}-\lambda_N)_++\delta$ instead of $\delta$ and $a=\lambda_N$ to get
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
        \frac{1}{N}g_\nu(\eta^\ast)\geqslant \ & \left(-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\log(2\beta)
        +\inf_{\gamma>\max(\lambda_N,\mu_{\rm max})+\delta}
        \left\{
        \beta \gamma -\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{i=1}^N \log(\gamma-\lambda_i)
        \right\}\right)\\
        &-\beta\abs{\lambda_N-\mu_{\rm max}}-\beta\delta.
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    Finally, using Lemma \ref{lem:sum_convergence_fixedR} for the expression in parentheses, we obtain
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}g_\nu(\eta^\ast)\geqslant F_{\mu}(\beta)-\frac{1}{\delta}W_1(\nu,\mu)-\beta\abs{\lambda_N-\mu_{\rm max}}-\beta\delta,
    \end{equation}
    and taking $\delta=\sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}$ finishes the proof.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
First, we note that
    \begin{equation}
        \hat{F}_{\nu}(\beta)\geqslant\frac{1}{N}\logE e^{-N\beta\max\limits_{1\leqslant i\leqslant N} \abs{\lambda_i}}\geqslant -\beta\Lambda,
    \end{equation}
    and by \Cref{cor:free_energy_trivial_bounds}
    \begin{equation}
        F_{\mu}(\beta)\leqslant\beta\abs{\mu_{\rm max}}\leqslant\beta\Lambda,
    \end{equation}
                                                                                                                  
    Therefore,
    \begin{equation}
        \hat{F}_{\nu}(\beta)-F_{\mu}(\beta)\geqslant -2\beta\Lambda.
    \end{equation}

    Now, for arbitrary $R>0$ and $\epsilon\in (0,1)$, define $I_{R,\epsilon}=[R^2(1-\epsilon),R^2(1+\epsilon)]$; later we pick $R=1$. By \eqref{eq:bound_on_BNk}, we have
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:fe_lower_bound_first_step}
        {\mathbb{E}}\left[
            \exp\left(\beta N\sum_{i=1}^N \lambda_i\frac{X_i^2}{\norm{X}^2}\right)
        \right]
        \geqslant
        {\mathbb{E}}\left[
            \exp\left(\frac{\beta}{R^2}\sum_{i=1}^N \lambda_iX_i^2\right)
            1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{ N^{-1}\norm{X}^2\in I_{R,\epsilon} }
        \right]
        \exp\left(
            -N \beta \Lambda \epsilon
        \right).
    \end{equation}
    For arbitrary $\eta\in\mathbb{R}$, for which the expectation in the denominator is finite, let us consider the measure ${\mathbb{Q}}_\eta$ defined in \eqref{eq:Q_def}. Using it to rewrite the expectation in the r.h.s. of \eqref{eq:fe_lower_bound_first_step} shows that the expectation is at least
    \begin{equation}
        e^{
            -NR^2\abs{\eta}\epsilon
        }\cdot
        {\mathbb{Q}}_\eta[N^{-1}\norm{X}^2\in I_{R,\epsilon}]
        \cdot{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\beta}{R^2}\sum_{i=1}^N \lambda_iX_i^2+\eta(\norm{X}^2-NR^2)\right)\right].
    \end{equation}
    
    Fix an arbitrary $\delta>0$ such that the conditions of \Cref{lem:away_from_lambda_max} are satisfied, and let $\eta^\ast$ be defined as in \Cref{lem:LB_cov_eta_star}. Using Chebyshev's inequality and \eqref{eq:Q_moment_bounds}, we get
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
        {\mathbb{Q}}_{\eta^\ast}[N^{-1}\norm{X}^2\in I_{R,\epsilon}]
        &=
        {\mathbb{Q}}_{\eta^\ast} \left[\abs{\norm{X}^2-{\mathbb{Q}}_{\eta^\ast}[\norm{X}^2]}\leqslant NR^2\epsilon\right]
        \\
        &\geqslant
        1
        -
        \frac{1}{N^2R^4\epsilon^2}
        \mathrm{Var}_{\eta^\ast}[\norm{X}^2]
        \\
        &\geqslant
        1
        -
        \frac{1}{2N\beta^2\delta^2\epsilon^2},
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    and so we conclude that
    \begin{equation}
        {\mathbb{Q}}_{\eta^\ast}[N^{-1}\norm{X}^2\in I_{R,\epsilon}]\geqslant\frac{1}{2},
    \end{equation}
    if we take
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:epsilon_def}
        \epsilon=\frac{1}{\beta\delta\sqrt{N}}
    \end{equation}
    with $N$ large enough for $\epsilon$ to be in $(0,1/2)$, i.e.
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:condition_on_N}
        N\geqslant\frac{4}{\beta^2\delta^2}.
    \end{equation}
    Therefore, picking $R=1$ we arrive at the bound
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}\log{\mathbb{E}}\left[\exp\left(\beta N\sum_{i=1}^N \lambda_i\frac{X_i^2}{\norm{X}^2}\right)\right]
        \geqslant -4\beta\Lambda\epsilon-\abs{\eta^\ast}\epsilon+\frac{1}{N}\log\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{N}g_{\hat{\mu}}(\eta^\ast)
    \end{equation}
    with $\epsilon$ given in \eqref{eq:epsilon_def}. Using \eqref{eq:eta_star_bounds}, we get
    \begin{equation}
        \abs{\eta^\ast}
        <
        \frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta\Lambda}{R^2}+\frac{1}{2R^2}.
    \end{equation}
    Hence, for $R=1$ and all $N\geqslant 4/(\beta^2\delta^2)$, we get
    \begin{equation}
        \hat{F}_{\nu}(\beta)\geqslant -\frac{1+5\beta\Lambda}{\beta\delta\sqrt{N}}-\frac{\log 2}{N}+\frac{1}{N}g_\nu(\eta^\ast).
    \end{equation}
    Using \Cref{lem:g_lower_bound}, we obtain
    \begin{equation}
        \hat{F}_{\nu}(\beta)\geqslantF_{\mu}(\beta)-\left(
        \frac{1+5\beta\Lambda}{\beta\delta\sqrt{N}}
        +
        \frac{\log 2}{N}
        +
        (1+\beta)\sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}
        +
        \beta\abs{\nu_{\max}-\mu_{\rm max}}
        \right),
    \end{equation}
    where $\delta>0$ is arbitrary for which the conditions of \Cref{lem:away_from_lambda_max} are satisfied.

    Finally, suppose that $\delta>0$ is such that $\varphi'_\mu(\mu_{\rm max} + 2\delta;\beta)<0$, but does not satisfy the conditions of \Cref{lem:away_from_lambda_max} or the inequality \eqref{eq:condition_on_N}. This means that at least one of the inequalities
    \begin{gather}
        \frac{2\abs{\nu_{\max}-\mu_{\rm max}}}{\delta}\geqslant 1,\\
        \frac{2W_1(\nu,\mu)}{\delta^2\abs{\varphi'_\mu(\mu_{\rm max} + 2\delta;\beta)}}\geqslant 1,\\
        \frac{4}{\beta^2\delta^2N}>1,
    \end{gather}
    holds. However, in this case
    \begin{equation}
        \left(\frac{2\abs{\nu_{\max}-\mu_{\rm max}}}{\delta}
        +
        \sqrt{\frac{2W_1(\nu,\mu)}{\delta^2\abs{\varphi'_\mu(\mu_{\rm max} + 2\delta;\beta)}}}
        +
        \frac{4}{\beta^2\delta^2N}\right)
        \cdot
        2\beta\Lambda
        \geqslant
        2\beta\Lambda,
    \end{equation}
    and so $\epsilon_\delta$ defined in \eqref{eq:epsilon_delta} is not less than the rough bound $2\beta\Lambda$.

    Thus, we get that for any $\delta>0$ such that $\varphi'_\mu(\mu_{\rm max} + 2\delta;\beta)<0$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:fe_lower_bound_bis}
        \hat{F}_{\nu}(\beta)\geqslant F_{\mu}(\beta)-\epsilon_\delta,
    \end{equation}
    where
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:epsilon_delta}
        \begin{aligned}
        \epsilon_\delta
        &=
        \frac{1+5\beta\Lambda}{\beta\delta\sqrt{N}}
        +
        \frac{\log 2}{N}
        +
        (1+\beta)\sqrt{W_1(\nu,\mu)}
        +
        \beta\abs{\lambda_N-\mu_{\rm max}}\\
        &+
        \left(\frac{2\abs{\lambda_N-\mu_{\rm max}}}{\delta}
        +
        \sqrt{\frac{2W_1(\nu,\mu)}{\delta^2\abs{\varphi'_\mu(\mu_{\rm max} + 2\delta;\beta)}}}
        +
        \frac{4}{\beta^2\delta^2N}\right)
        2\beta\Lambda.
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
                                                                                                                  
    The statement of the lemma then follows trivially.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
The first statement of the theorem follows directly from Lemmas~\ref{lem:fe_upper_bound} and~\ref{lem:fe_lower_bound}.

    It remains to prove the uniform convergence assertion.
    Assumption \ref{main_assumption} implies that the spectra are uniformly bounded,
    that $W_1(\mu_N,\mu)\to0$, and that
    $\lambda_N\to\mu_{\rm max}$.
    The preceding estimates therefore give
    \begin{equation}
        \widehat F_{\mu_N}(\beta)\longrightarrow F_\mu(\beta)
        \qquad\text{for every }\beta\in(0,\beta_c).
    \end{equation}
    The same holds at $\beta=0$, since both functions vanish there.
    
    Moreover, for all $s,t\geqslant0$,
    \begin{equation}
        |\widehat F_{\mu_N}(t)-\widehat F_{\mu_N}(s)|
        \leqslant\Lambda|t-s|,
    \end{equation}
    while Lemma~2.5 gives
    \begin{equation}
        |F_\mu(t)-F_\mu(s)|\leqslant\Lambda|t-s|.
    \end{equation}
    Hence, for every $\eta\in(0,\beta_c)$,
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
        |\widehat F_{\mu_N}(\beta_c)-F_\mu(\beta_c)|
        &\leqslant
        |\widehat F_{\mu_N}(\beta_c-\eta)
          -F_\mu(\beta_c-\eta)|
          +2\Lambda\eta.
    \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    Taking first $N\to\infty$ and then $\eta\downarrow0$ proves
    convergence at $\beta_c$. Finally, the common Lipschitz bound
    and a finite-net argument imply
    \begin{equation}
        \sup_{\beta\in[0,\beta_c]}
        |\widehat F_{\mu_N}(\beta)-F_\mu(\beta)|
        \longrightarrow0.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:sec4main}
    There exists $c>0$ such that for any $\beta>\beta_c$, $0<\epsilon<\min\{q^*,1-q^*\}$, $\tau_L\in [0,1-q^*]$, $\tau_R\in [0,q^*]$ and $K=1,\dots N-2$
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}
        \log
        Z_N (\beta,A_{K,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R})
        \leqslant
        \sup_{x,y\in C_\epsilon(\tau_L,\tau_R)} M_{N,K}(x,y)
        +
        c\frac{\log N \epsilon^{-1}}{N}
        +
        6\beta\Lambda\epsilon,
    \end{equation}

    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}
        \log
        Z_N (\beta,A_{0,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R})
        \leqslant
        \sup_{x,y\in C_\epsilon(\tau_L,\tau_R)} M_{N,0}(x,0)
        +
        c\frac{\log N \epsilon^{-1}}{N}
        +
        6\beta\Lambda\epsilon,
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}
        \log
        Z_N (\beta,A_{N-1,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R})
        \leqslant
        \sup_{x,y\in C_\epsilon(\tau_L,\tau_R)} M_{N,N-1}(0,y)
        +
        c\frac{\log N \epsilon^{-1}}{N}
        +
        6\beta\Lambda\epsilon,
    \end{equation}
    where

    \begin{equation}\label{eq:simplex_tauL_tau_R}
        C_{\epsilon}(\tau_L,\tau_R)
        =
        \left\{
        x,y\in[0,1-q^*]\times [0,q^*]: \ \abs{x-\tau_L}\leqslant\epsilon, \  \abs{y-\tau_R}\leqslant\epsilon 
        \right\}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
\label{lem:Z_Nslice_decompo}
    For any $\beta>0$, $\tau_L\in [0,1-q^*]$, $\tau_R\in [0,q^*]$ and $K=0,\dots N-1$ such that $A_{K,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R}$ has non-zero measure
    \begin{equation}
        \left|
        \frac{1}{N}
        \log
        Z_N (\beta,A_{K,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R})
        -
                                                                                           
        \tilde{M}_{N,K}(\tau_L,\tau_R)
        -
        \frac{1}{N}\log E \left[ A_{K,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R} \right]
        \right|
        \leqslant
        6\beta\Lambda\epsilon.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
We start by fixing some $K=1,\dots,N-2$. Define the $\sigma$-algebra ${\mathcal F}=\sigma(\tau_{L,K}(\sigma),\sigma_{N-K}^2,\tau_{R,K}(\sigma))$, and note that
    \begin{equation}
        Z_N(\beta,A_{K,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R})
        =
        E\left[
            E[e^{\beta H_N(\sigma)}\mid{\mathcal F}]
            1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{A_{K,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R}(\sigma)}
        \right].
    \end{equation}
    Under $E[\cdot| {\mathcal F}]$, the random variable $\sigma_{N-K}^2$ is a.s. constant and $(\sigma_i)_{i=1}^{N-K-1}$ and $(\sigma_i)_{i=N-K+1}^N$ are independent. Thus
    \begin{equation}
        E[e^{\beta H_N(\sigma)}\mid{\mathcal F}]
        =
        E\left[e^{N\beta\sum_{i=1}^{N-K-1} \lambda_i\sigma_i^2}\mid{\mathcal F}\right]
        e^{N\beta\lambda_{N-K}\sigma_{N-K}^2}
        E\left[e^{ N\beta\sum_{i=N-K+1}^N \lambda_i\sigma_i^2}\mid{\mathcal F}\right].
    \end{equation}
    Furthermore, under $E[\cdot| {\mathcal F}]$ the vectors $(\sigma_i)_{i=1}^{N-K-1}$ and $(\sigma_i)_{i=N-K+1}^N$ are uniform on $S_{N-K-2}(\tau_{L,K}^{1/2})$ and $S_{K-1}(\tau_{R,K}^{1/2})$ respectively. Thus    
    \begin{equation}
        E\left[e^{N\beta\sum_{i=1}^{N-K-1} \lambda_i\sigma_i^2}\mid{\mathcal F}\right]
        =
        E_{N-K-2}\left[ e^{ N \beta \tau_{L,K} \sum_{i=1}^{N-K-1} \lambda_i\sigma_i^2} \right]
        \overset{\eqref{eq:F_nu_def},\eqref{eq:def_mu_hatLR}}{=}
        e^{(N-K-1)\hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{L,K}}\left(\frac{N}{N-K-1} \beta \tau_{L,K} \right)},
    \end{equation}
    and similarly
    \begin{equation}
        E\left[e^{ N\beta\sum_{i=N-K+1}^N \lambda_i\sigma_i^2}\mid{\mathcal F}\right]
        \overset{\eqref{eq:F_nu_def},\eqref{eq:def_mu_hatLR}}{=}
        e^{K \hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{R,K}}\left(\frac{N}{K} \beta \tau_{R,K} \right)}.
    \end{equation}    
    Combining the above and using that $\sigma_{N-K}^2 = 1 - \tau_{L,K} - \tau_{R,K}$ we obtain
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:ZNA}
        Z_N(\beta,A_{K,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R})
        =
        E\left[
            e^{N\tilde{M}_{N,K}(\tau_{L,K}(\sigma),\tau_{R,K}(\sigma))
            }
        1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{A_{K,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R}}(\sigma)
        \right].
    \end{equation}
    The proof of the lemma for $K=1,\dots,N-2$ is complete by recalling that $\tilde{M}_{N,K}$ is $3\beta\Lambda$-Lipschitz. Indeed, $x\to\frac{N-K-1}{N}\hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{L,K}}\left(\frac{N}{N-K-1}\beta x\right)$ and $x\mapsto \frac{K}{N}\hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{R,K}}\left(\frac{N}{K}\beta x\right)$
    are $\beta\Lambda$-Lipschitz. Then, from the definition \eqref{eq:def_tildeMNK}, $\tilde{M}_{N,K}$ is $3\beta\Lambda$-Lipschitz. The proof for $K=0,N-1$ is analogous, using the corresponding definition of $\tilde{M}_{N,K}$.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- lemma-22: body_md -->
\label{lem:measure_AK_bulk}
    There exits $c>0$ such that for any $0<\epsilon<\min(q^*,1-q^*)$, $\tau_L\in [0,1-q^*]$, $\tau_R\in [0,q^*]$ and $K=1,\dots N-2$
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}\log E \left[ A_{K,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R} \right]
        \leqslant
        - 
        \frac{1}{2}D(\alpha||u_L,u_R)
        +
        c\frac{\log N \epsilon^{-1}}{N},
    \end{equation}
    where $u_L=\min(\tau_L+\epsilon,1-q^*)$ and $u_R=\min(\tau_R+\epsilon,q^*)$.
<!-- end lemma-22 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
We start by noting that if $\tau_{L,K}(\sigma),\tau_{R,K}(\sigma)$ have joint density $p_{N,K}(x,y)$ under $E$ then
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:measure_set_as_integral}
        E \left[ A_{K,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R}\right]
        =
        \iint\limits_{ \substack{\abs{x-\tau_L}\leqslant\epsilon, \ x\leqslant 1-q^*\\ \abs{y-\tau_R}\leqslant\epsilon, \ y\leqslant q^*}} p_{N,K}(x,y) dx dy.
    \end{equation}
    For $K=1,\dots,N-2$ 
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:qL_qR_density}
        p_{N,K}(x,y)
        =
        C_{N,K} x^{\frac{N-K-3}{2}}y^{\frac{K-2}{2}}(1-x-y)^{-\frac12} 1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{\{x>0; \ y>0; \ x+y < 1\}},
    \end{equation}
    (see \cite[Lemma~8]{BK} or \cite[Theorem~1]{harman2010}), where
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:C_NK_def}
        C_{N,K}
        =
        \frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{N-K-1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{K}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)}.
    \end{equation}
    A control of $\tfrac{1}{N}\log C_{N,K}$ is given at the end of this section, in Lemma \ref{lem:bound_CNK}. Because both $u_L\geqslant \epsilon$ and $u_R\geqslant \epsilon$ we obtain, for $x,y$ in the integration domain of \eqref{eq:measure_set_as_integral}

    \begin{equation}
         p_{N,K}(x,y)
        \leqslant
        C_{N,K} \cdot
        \epsilon^{-3}
        u_L^{(N-K)/2}u_R^{K/2} \cdot
        \frac{\xi_{K,N}(x,y)}{\sqrt{1-x-y}} 1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{\{x+y < 1\}}.
    \end{equation}
    where 

    \begin{equation}
        \xi_{K,N}(x,y)
        =
        \begin{cases}
            1 \text{ if } 2\leqslant K \leqslant N-3,\\
            x^{-1/2} \text{ if } K=N-2,\\
            y^{-1/2} \text{ if } K=1.
        \end{cases}
    \end{equation}
    Using Lemma \ref{lem:bound_CNK} and that there exists $c>0$ such that   \begin{equation}\label{eq:uniform_bound_inverse_root_2D_integral}
        \int_0^t\int_0^{1-t}\frac{\xi_{K,N}(x,y)}{\sqrt{1-x-y}}  dx dy \leqslant c \quad \forall t \in[0,1],
    \end{equation} yields
    
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}\log E \left[ A_{K,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R} \right]
        \leqslant
        - 
        \frac{1}{2}D(\alpha||u_L,u_R)
        +
        c\frac{\log N \epsilon^{-1}}{N},
    \end{equation}
    for some possibly larger constant $c$.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- lemma-23: body_md -->
\label{lem:measure_AK_K=0_N}
    There exits $c>0$ such that for any $0<\epsilon<\min(q^*,1-q^*)$, $\tau_L\in \pi_L$ and $\tau_R\in \pi_R$ 
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}\log E \left[ A_{0,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R} \right]
        \leqslant
        - 
        \frac{1}{2}D(0||u_L,0)
        +
        c\frac{\log N \epsilon^{-1}}{N},
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}\log E \left[ A_{N-1,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R} \right]
        \leqslant
        - 
        \frac{1}{2}D(1||0,u_R)
        +
        c\frac{\log N \epsilon^{-1}}{N}
    \end{equation}
    where $u_L=\min(\tau_L+\epsilon,1-q^*)$ and $u_R=\min(\tau_R+\epsilon,q^*)$.
<!-- end lemma-23 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
If $\tau_R>\epsilon$ the proof is trivial for $K=0$, while for $\tau_L>\epsilon$ it is trivial for $K=N-1$. Indeed, in this range the sets $A_{0,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R}$ and $A_{N-1,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,\tau_R}$ are empty by \eqref{eq:defAkqlqrdelta} and \eqref{eq:def_q_tau_LR_convention}. In the following, fix $K=0$, $\tau_R=0$. Recall that $\tau_{R,0}=0$ a.s and that under $E$, $\tau_{L,K}$ has density (see \cite[Lemma~8]{BK} or \cite[Theorem~1]{harman2010})
    \begin{equation}
        p_{N,0}(\tau_L)
            =
            \dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{N-1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)}
            \tau_L^{\frac{N-3}{2}}
            (1-\tau_L)^{-\frac12}
            1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{\{0<\tau_L< 1\}}.
    \end{equation}
    Omitting details, using \eqref{eq:measure_set_as_integral} as in Lemma \ref{lem:measure_AK_bulk} yields
    
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}\log E \left[ A_{0,\epsilon}^{\tau_L,0} \right]
        \leqslant
        - 
        \frac{1}{2}D(0||u_L,0)
        +
        c\frac{\log N \epsilon^{-1}}{N},
    \end{equation}
    and concludes the proof of the lemma for $K=0$. The result for $K=N-1$ follows analogously.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
The proof follows from combining Lemma \ref{lem:Z_Nslice_decompo}, \ref{lem:measure_AK_bulk} and \ref{lem:measure_AK_K=0_N}.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- lemma-24: body_md -->
\label{lem:bound_CNK}
    There exists a positive constant $c$ such that for any $N$ and $K\in [0,N-1]$,
    \begin{equation}
        \left|
        \frac{1}{N}\log C_{N,K}
        +
        \frac{1-\alpha}{2}
        \log (1-\alpha)
        +
        \frac{\alpha}{2}
        \log \alpha
        \right|
        \leqslant
        \frac{c}{N}\log N,
    \end{equation}
    where for $K=1,\dots,N-2$ $C_{N,K}$ is defined in \eqref{eq:C_NK_def} and $C_{N,0}=C_{N,N-1}=\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{N}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{N-1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)}$ .
<!-- end lemma-24 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
By Stirling's formula, there exist positive universal constant $c_1$ and $c_2$ such that $c_1\leqslant\Gamma(x)/(x^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\frac{x}{e})^{x})\leqslant c_2$
    for all $x\geqslant\frac{1}{2}$. Thus letting $\delta=N^{-1}$, we have for $K=1,\dots,N-2$
    \begin{equation}
        \left|
        \frac{1}{N}\log C_{N,K}
        +
        \frac12
        (1 - \alpha - \delta)
        \log (1-\alpha - \delta)
        +
        \frac12
        \alpha
        \log \alpha
        \right|
        \leqslant
        \frac{c}{N}\log N
    \end{equation}
    for $\alpha=K/N \leqslant 1 - 2\delta$. Using that $x \to (1-x) \log (1-x)$ is $L$-Lipschitz on $[0,1-\delta]$, with constant $L=1 + \log \delta^{-1}$ concludes the proof of the lemma for $K=1,\dots,N-2$. The proof for $K=0$, $K=N-1$ follows analogously.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:aux}
 For any $B>\beta_c$ and $\beta \in (\beta_c,B]$
    \begin{equation}    
        F_N(\beta)
        \leqslant
        \sup_{(\alpha,\tau_L,\tau_R) \in \mathfrak{S}_{\beta}}
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,\tau_L,\tau_R,\alpha)
        +
        \mathrm{Err}_N(B,\mu_N),
    \end{equation}
    where $M_\mathrm{TAP}$ is defined in \eqref{eq:def_ftap} and $\mathrm{Err}_N(B,\mu_N)$ goes to $0$ with $N$.
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- lemma-25: body_md -->
\label{lem:sec5main}
    For any $\beta>0$, $ 0 \leqslant K\leqslant N-1$ and  $\tau_L\in[0,1-q^*]$, $\tau_R\in [0,q^*]$ with $\tau_R=0$ when $K=0$, and $\tau_L=0$ when $K=N-1$, it holds that
    \begin{equation}
        M_{N,K}(\tau_L,\tau_R)
        \leqslant
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta, \tau_L, \tau_R, \alpha)
        +
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(5)}
        \big(\beta,\mu_N\big),
    \end{equation}
    where $\alpha=K/N$ if $K=0,\dots,N-2$, $\alpha=1$ if $K=N-1$, and $\mathrm{Err}_N^{(5)}$ is given by 
    
    \begin{equation}
    \begin{aligned}
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(5)} \big(\beta,\mu_N\big)
        =& \ 
        \frac{3\beta\Lambda}{\sqrt{N}}
        +
        256 (\beta\Lambda+1)^2 \sqrt{\mathrm{Err}_N^{(*)}(\mu_N,\mu)}
        +
        \beta\mathrm{Err}_N^{(**)} \big(\mu_N\big).
    \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-25 -->
<!-- lemma-26: body_md -->
\label{lem:FL_UB_delta_macro}
    For any $\beta>0$, $\delta>2/N$, $0 \leqslant K\leqslant (1-\delta)N$ and $\tau_L\in(0,1-q^*]$ we have
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{N-K-1}{N}\hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{L,K}}\left(
            \frac{N}{N-K-1}\beta \tau_L
        \right)
        \leqslant
                                                                                                                                        
        (1-\alpha)
        F_{\mu_{L,\alpha}}
        \left(
            \frac{ \beta \tau_L }{1-\alpha}
        \right)
        +
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(1)} \big(\beta,\delta,\mu_N\big)
        ,
    \end{equation}
    where $\alpha=K/N\leqslant 1-\delta$  and the error term $\mathrm{Err}_N^{(1)}$ is given by
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:lemma_FLK_main_regime}
        \begin{aligned}
            \mathrm{Err}_N^{(1)} \big(\beta,\delta,\mu_N\big)
            =&
            \frac{1}{2N}
            +
            \frac{2\Lambda \beta}{\delta N}
            +
            C_{\beta/\delta}
            \left(\frac{\beta}{\delta}+1\right)
            \mathrm{Err}_N^{(*)}(\mu_N,\mu)
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    for $C_{\beta/\delta}=128[1+\beta\Lambda/\delta]e^{8(1+\beta\Lambda/\delta)}$.
<!-- end lemma-26 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
We apply Lemma \ref{lem:fe_upper_bound} with $\nu=\hat{\mu}_{L,K}$, $\mu = \mu_{L,\alpha}$, $N-K-1$ in place of $N$, and $\frac{N}{N-K-1}\beta\tau_L$ in place of $\beta$. This yields
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{N-K-1}{N}\hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{L,K}}\left(\frac{N \beta \tau_L}{N-K-1}\right)
        \leqslant
                                                                                                                                          
        (1-\alpha')F_{\mu_{L,\alpha}}
        \left(
            \frac{\beta \tau_L}{1-\alpha'}
        \right)
        +
        (1-\alpha')\mathrm{Err}_N,
    \end{equation}
    where $\alpha' = \frac{K+1}{N}$ and the error term $\mathrm{Err}_N$ is given by
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \mathrm{Err}_N
            = & \            
            C
            \left(
                \tfrac{\beta\tau_L}{1-\alpha'} + 1
            \right) \left(
                \sqrt{W_1\left(\hat{\mu}_{L,K}, \mu_{L,\alpha}\right)} 
                +
                \left|
                    \lambda_{N-K-1}
                    -
                    I(1-\alpha)
                \right|
                +
                \sqrt{\tfrac{1}{(1-\alpha')N}}
            \right).
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    It remains to use that $F_{\mu_{L,\alpha}}(z)$ is both bounded by $z\Lambda $ and $\Lambda$-Lipschitz to get

    \begin{equation}\label{eq:FLalpha_lipschitz}
        (1-\alpha')F_{\mu_{L,\alpha}}
        \left(\frac{\beta \tau_L}{1-\alpha'}\right)
        -
        (1-\alpha)F_{\mu_{L,\alpha}}
        \left(\frac{\beta \tau_L}{1-\alpha}\right)
        \leqslant
        \frac{2\Lambda \beta}{\delta N}.
    \end{equation}
    To conclude, note that
    
    \begin{equation}
        \left|
        \lambda_{N-K-1}
        -
        I(1-\alpha)
        \right|
        \leqslant
        \sup_{1\leqslant k \leqslant N} |\lambda_k-I(k/N)|
        +
        \sup_{ |h-u|\leqslant 1/N} |I(h)-I(u)|,
    \end{equation}
    and use the triangle inequality: $W_1\left(\hat{\mu}_{L,K}, \mu_{L,\alpha}\right)\leqslant W_1\left(\hat{\mu}_{L,K}, \mu_{L,\alpha'}\right)+W_1\left(\mu_{L,\alpha'}, \mu_{L,\alpha}\right)$. It is a direct consequence of \cite[equation 2.48]{villani2021topics} that
    \begin{equation}
        (1-\alpha')W_1\left(\hat{\mu}_{L,K}, \mu_{L,\alpha'}\right)\leqslant W_1\left(\mu_N, \mu\right),
    \end{equation}
    while a simple coupling argument yields $W_1\left(\mu_{L,\alpha'}, \mu_{L,\alpha}\right)\leqslant \frac{2\Lambda}{(1-\alpha')N}$,
    leading to the error bound $\mathrm{Err}^{(1)}$.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- lemma-27: body_md -->
\label{lem:FR_UB_delta_macro}
    For any $\beta>0$, $\delta>0$, $\delta N\leqslant K\leqslant N-1$, $\tau_R\in [0,q^*]$
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{K}{N}\hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{R,K}}\left( \frac{N}{K}\beta \tau_R \right) 
        \leqslant
                                                                                                                           
        \alpha
        F_{\mu_{R,\alpha}}
        \left(
            \frac{ \beta \tau_R }{\alpha}
        \right)
        +
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(2)}\big(\beta,\delta,\mu_N\big),
    \end{equation}
    where $\alpha=K/N\geqslant \delta$ and the error term $\mathrm{Err}_N^{(2)}$ is given by
    \begin{equation}
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(2)} \big(\beta,\delta,\mu_N\big)
        =
         C_{\beta/\delta} \left(\frac{\beta}{\delta}+1\right)
         \mathrm{Err}_N^{(*)}(\mu_N,\mu)
    \end{equation}
    for $C_{\beta/\delta}=128[1+\beta\Lambda/\delta]e^{8(1+\beta\Lambda/\delta)}$.
<!-- end lemma-27 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
The proof is similar to that of Lemma \ref{lem:FL_UB_delta_macro}, without the substitution of $\alpha'$ by $\alpha$. Hence, we do not give all details.  We apply Lemma \ref{lem:fe_upper_bound} with $\nu=\hat{\mu}_{R,K}$, $\mu = \mu_{R,\alpha}$, $K$ in place of $N$, and $\frac{N}{K}\beta\tau_R$ in place of $\beta$.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- lemma-28: body_md -->
\label{lem:FL_UB_delta_micro}
    For any $\beta>0$, $2/N<\delta< \mu \big( [\mu_{\rm min}, \mu_{\rm min}+1]\big)$, $(1-\delta)N \leqslant K\leqslant N-2$ and $\tau_L\in(0,1-q^*]$ we have
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{N-K-1}{N}\hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{L,K}}\left(
            \frac{N}{N-K-1}\beta \tau_L
        \right)
        \leqslant
                                                                                                                                        
        (1-\alpha)
        F_{\mu_{L,\alpha}}
        \left(
            \frac{ \beta \tau_L }{1-\alpha}
        \right)
        +
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(3)} \big(\beta,\delta,\mu_N\big)
        ,
    \end{equation}
    where $\alpha=K/N\geqslant 1-\delta$  and the error term $\mathrm{Err}_N^{(3)}$ is given by
    \begin{equation}
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(3)} \big(\beta,\delta,\mu_N\big)
        =
        \beta  | \lambda_{\lceil \delta N \rceil}-\lambda_1| 
        +
        \beta  | \lambda_{1}-\mu_{\rm min}|
                                                                                 
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-28 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
First, from the definition \eqref{eq:F_nu_def}, for any $\beta>0$, any $1\leqslant K\leqslant N-1$ and any $\tau_L\in (0,1-q^*]$ we have
    
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:adhoc_FL_UB}
         \frac{N-K-1}{N}\hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{L,K}}\left(
            \frac{N}{N-K-1}\beta \tau_L
        \right)
        \leqslant
        \beta \tau_L \lambda_{\lceil \delta N \rceil}.
    \end{equation}
    Next, Corollary \ref{cor:free_energy_trivial_bounds}                                                                                                                             
    evaluated at $\mu=\mu_{L,\alpha}$ and $z=\tfrac{\beta\tau_L}{1-\alpha}$ yields

    \begin{equation}
        (1-\alpha)F_{\mu_{L,\alpha}}
        \left(
            \frac{ \beta \tau_L }{1-\alpha}
        \right)
        \geqslant 
        \beta \tau_L \mu_{\rm min},
    \end{equation}
    where we used that the support of $\mu=\mu_{L,\alpha}$ is $[\mu_{\rm min},I(1-\alpha)]$. The proof of the lemma follows.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- lemma-29: body_md -->
\label{lem:FR_UB}
    For any $\beta>0$, any $0<\delta\leqslant \mu\big([\mu_{\rm max}-1,\mu_{\rm max}]\big)$, any $1\leqslant K\leqslant \delta N$ and $\tau_R\in [0,q^*]$, letting $\alpha=K/N\leqslant \delta$ we have
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{K}{N}\hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{R,K}}\left(\frac{N}{K}\beta \tau_R\right)
        \leqslant
                                                                                                                           
        \alphaF_{\mu_{R,\alpha}}( \beta \tau_R/\alpha ) +
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(4)} \big(\beta,\delta,\mu_N\big),
    \end{equation}
    where the error term $\mathrm{Err}_N^{(4)}$ is given by

    \begin{equation}
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(4)}\big(\beta,\delta,\mu_N\big)
        =
        \beta  |\lambda_N-\mu_{\rm max}|
        +
        \beta  |\mu_{\rm max}-I(1-\delta)|
                                                                               
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-29 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
First, from the definition \eqref{eq:F_nu_def}, for any $\beta>0$, any $1\leqslant K\leqslant N-1$ and any $\tau_R\in [0,q^*]$ we have
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:adhoc_FR_UB}
        \frac{K}{N}\hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{R,K}}\left(\frac{N}{K}\beta \tau_R\right)\leqslant \beta \tau_R \lambda_N.
    \end{equation}
    Next, Corollary \ref{cor:free_energy_trivial_bounds} evaluated at $\mu=\mu_{R,\alpha}$ and $z=\tfrac{\beta \tau_R}{\alpha}$ yields

    \begin{equation}
        \alpha F_{\mu_{R,\alpha}}\left( \frac{\beta \tau_R}{\alpha} \right)
        \geqslant
        \beta \tau_R I(1-\alpha),
    \end{equation}
    where we used that the support of $\mu=\mu_{R,\alpha}$ is $[I(1-\alpha),\mu_{\rm max}]$. The proof of the lemma follows.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
First, fix some $2/N<\delta< \mu \big( [\mu_{\rm min}, \mu_{\rm min}+1]\big)\wedge\mu\big([\mu_{\rm max}-1,\mu_{\rm max}]\big)$. We start by treating the case $K\in [1,N-2]$. In the definition of $M_{N,K}$ given in \eqref{eq:def_MNK}, the terms $\hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{L,K}}$ and $\hat{F}_{\hat{\mu}_{R,K}}$ appear. Depending on the range of $\alpha=K/N$, we proved the corresponding upper bounds in Lemmas \ref{lem:FL_UB_delta_macro}, \ref{lem:FR_UB_delta_macro}, \ref{lem:FL_UB_delta_micro} and \ref{lem:FR_UB}. By combining these bounds and recognizing the expression \eqref{eq:def_ftap} in the resulting bound, we obtain, for $\tau_L\in[0,1-q^*]$ and $\tau_R\in[0,q^*]$   \begin{equation}\label{eq:Mn_is_Ftap_minus_CNK}
        M_{N,K}(\tau_L,\tau_R)
        \leqslant
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta, \tau_L, \tau_R, \alpha)
        +
        \sum_{i=1}^4 \mathrm{Err}_N^{(i)}(\beta,\delta,\mu_N).
    \end{equation}
    
    Turning to the case $K=0$, we similarly apply Lemma \ref{lem:FL_UB_delta_macro} to the r.h.s. of \eqref{eq:def_MN0}, recognize the definition of $M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,\tau_L,\tau_R,0)$ from \eqref{eq:def_ftap_alpha=0} and conclude that    \begin{equation}\label{eq:Mn_is_Ftap_minus_CN0}
        M_{N,0}(\tau_L)
        \leqslant
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta, \tau_L, \tau_R, 0)
        +
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(1)} \big(\beta,\delta,\mu_N\big).
    \end{equation}
    The same holds for $K=N-1$, and combining results we prove that

    \begin{equation}
        M_{N,K}(\tau_L,\tau_R)
        \leqslant
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta, \tau_L, \tau_R, \alpha)
        +
        \sum_{i=1}^4\mathrm{Err}_N^{(i)} \big(\beta,\delta,\mu_N\big),
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:def_err7}
    \begin{aligned}
        \sum_{i=1}^4\mathrm{Err}_N^{(i)} \big(.\big)
        = & \ 
       \frac{1}{2N}
        +
        \frac{2\Lambda \beta}{\delta N}
        +
        256[1+\beta\Lambda/\delta] e^{8(1+\beta\Lambda/\delta)}
        \left(\frac{\beta}{\delta}+1\right)
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(*)}(\mu_N,\mu)\\
        &+
        \beta \left(  | \lambda_{\lceil \delta N \rceil}-\lambda_1| 
        +
        | \lambda_{1}-\mu_{\rm min}|
        +
        |\lambda_N-\mu_{\rm max}|
        +
        |\mu_{\rm max}-I(1-\delta)|
        \right).
    \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    It remains to choose $\delta_N=\log(1/\mathrm{Err}_N^{(*)}(\mu_N,\mu))^{-1/2}\vee\frac1{\sqrt N}$ to conclude the proof of the lemma. Remark that this choice verifies $2/N<\delta_N<\mu \big( [\mu_{\rm min}, \mu_{\rm min}+1]\big)\wedge\mu\big([\mu_{\rm max}-1,\mu_{\rm max}]\big)$ for $N$ large enough because $\mathrm{Err}_N^{(*)}(\mu_N,\mu)$ vanishes (see Lemma \ref{lem:error_term_goes_to_zero}).
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
We start by fixing some $\beta>\frac{\beta_c}{1-1/N}$ and $2/N<\delta<\mu \big( [\mu_{\rm min}, \mu_{\rm min}+1]\big)\wedge\mu\big([\mu_{\rm max}-1,\mu_{\rm max}]\big)$. Then, we fix $\epsilon=1/N$ (which satisfies $\epsilon\leqslant q^*$ from the choice of $\beta$) and recall \eqref{eq:covering_sphere_AK}, that is, a collection of at most $N^3$ sets of the form $A_{K,1/N}^{\tau_L,\tau_R}$ covers $S^{N-1}$. Then, putting together the results of Proposition \ref{prop:sec4main} and Lemma \ref{lem:sec5main} leads to 
    \begin{equation}
        F_N(\beta)
        \leqslant
        \sup_{(\alpha,\tau_L,\tau_R) \in \mathfrak{S}_{\beta}}
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,\tau_L,\tau_R,\alpha)
        +
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(5)} \big(\beta,\mu_N\big)
        +
        \frac{c}{N}\log N.
    \end{equation}
    for $\beta_c\leqslant\beta\leqslant\frac{\beta_c}{1-1/N}$, use Theorem \ref{thm:sec2_main} to get

    \begin{equation}
        F_N(\beta)
        \leqslant
        F_{\mu}(\beta)+\mathrm{Err}^{(*)}(\mu_N\mu)
        =
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,1-q^*,0,0)+\mathrm{Err}^{(*)}(\mu_N\mu).
    \end{equation}
    Because $\mathrm{Err}_N^{(5)} \big(\beta,\mu_N\big)+\mathrm{Err}_N^{(*)} \big(\mu_N,\mu\big)
    +
    \frac{c}{N}\log N$ is increasing in $\beta$, fixing

    \begin{equation}
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(6)}(B,\mu_N)
        = 
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(5)} \big(B,\mu_N\big)
        +
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(*)} \big(\mu_N,\mu\big)
        +
        \frac{c}{N}\log N.
    \end{equation}
    concludes the proof of Proposition \ref{prop:aux}.
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- lemma-30: body_md -->
\label{lem:error_term_goes_to_zero}
    The master error term $\mathrm{Err}_N^{(*)}$ defined in \eqref{eq:def_master_error} goes to $0$.
<!-- end lemma-30 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
Recalling the definition \eqref{eq:def_master_error}, we start by noting that $\sup_{ |h-u|\leqslant 1/N} |I(h)-I(u)|$ is the modulus of continuity, because $I$ is absolutely continuous it goes to $0$. Then, it suffices to check that

    \begin{equation}
        \lim_{N\to \infty}\sup_{1\leqslant k\leqslant N} \left\{
        \left| \lambda_{k}-I(k/N) \right| \right\}
        =
        0.
    \end{equation}
    We use \cite[Theorem 1.2]{bogoya2016convergence}, with the caveat that their result only holds if the $\lambda_i$ are all in the support of $\mu$. We deal with this by considering the modified eigenvalues $\tilde{\lambda}_i$ defined by

    \begin{equation}
    \tilde{\lambda}_i
    =
    \begin{cases}
        \mu_{\rm min} \quad \text{if } \lambda_i<\mu_{\rm min}\\
        \mu_{\rm max} \quad \text{if } \lambda_i>\mu_{\rm max}\\
        \lambda_i \quad \text{otherwise.}
    \end{cases}
    \end{equation}
    Doing so, \cite[Theorem 1.2]{bogoya2016convergence} can be applied to obtain
    
    \begin{equation}
        \lim_{N\to \infty}\sup_{1\leqslant k\leqslant N} \left\{
        \left| \tilde{\lambda}_{k}-I(k/N) \right| \right\}
        =
        0.
    \end{equation}
    It remains to note that

    \begin{equation}
        \sup_{1\leqslant k\leqslant N} \left\{
        \left| \lambda_{k}-I(k/N) \right| \right\}
        \leqslant
        \sup_{1\leqslant k\leqslant N} \left\{
        \left| \tilde{\lambda}_{k}-I(k/N) \right| \right\}
        +
        |\lambda_1-\mu_{\rm min}|
        +
        |\lambda_N-\mu_{\rm max}|
    \end{equation}
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- lemma-31: body_md -->
\label{lem:ftap_explicit_pre}
    For any $\beta>0, \ \alpha \in (0,1)$ and $\tau_L,\tau_R>0$ s.t. $\tau_L+\tau_R\leqslant1$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:def_ftap_explicit_pre}
    \begin{aligned}
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta, \tau_L, \tau_R, \alpha)
        = &\ 
        -\frac{1}{2}
        -
        \frac{1}{2} \log (2\beta)        
        -
        (1-\alpha)L^*_{\mu_{L,\alpha}}\left(\frac{\beta\tau_L}{1-\alpha}\right)
        -
        \alpha L^*_{\mu_{R,\alpha}}\left(\frac{\beta\tau_R}{\alpha}\right)\\
        &+
        \beta I(1-\alpha) 
        (1 - \tau_L - \tau_R).
    \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
         
                                             
                                                     
           
           
                     
           
                                   
           
                                    
           
                                     
                    
         
    Also for $\alpha=0,\tau_L\in(0,1]$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:f_TAP_q_star_alpha=0_pre}
    \begin{aligned}
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta, \tau_L, 0, 0)
        =& \ 
        -
        \frac{1}{2}
        -
        \frac{1}{2} \log (2\beta)
        -L^*_{\mu_{L,\alpha}}\left(\beta\tau_L\right)
        +
        \beta \mu_{\rm max} (1-\tau_L).
    \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-31 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
The claim \eqref{eq:def_ftap_explicit_pre} follows from substituting \eqref{eq:F_def} into \eqref{eq:def_ftap}, and noting that some of the resulting terms cancel $-\frac{1}{2} D(\alpha||\tau_L,\tau_R)$ (recall \eqref{eq:def_entropy}). The claim \eqref{eq:f_TAP_q_star_alpha=0_pre} similarly follows from \eqref{eq:def_ftap_alpha=0}, \eqref{eq:F_def} and \eqref{eq:def_entropy_alpha=0}.
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- lemma-32: body_md -->
\label{lem:ident_F_star_LR_G_LR}
    For all $\beta,\tau>0$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:ident_F_star_LR_G_LR}
        -(1-\alpha)L^*_{\mu_{L,\alpha}}\left(\frac{\beta\tau}{1-\alpha}\right)
        \overset{\forall \alpha\in[0,1)}{=}
        G_L(\beta,\tau,\alpha),
        \qquad
        -\alpha L^*_{\mu_{R,\alpha}}\left(\frac{\beta\tau}{\alpha}\right)
        \overset{\forall \alpha\in(0,1]}{=}
        G_R(\beta,\tau,\alpha).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-32 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
Recall that $\mu_{L,\alpha}(dx)=\frac{1}{1-\alpha}1_{[\mu_{\rm min},I(1-\alpha)]}\mu(dx)$
    and $\mu_{R,\alpha}(dx)=\frac{1}{\alpha}1_{[I(1-\alpha),\mu_{\rm max}]}\mu(dx)$. Using this, \eqref{eq:def_log_potential}, \eqref{eq:LegendreLstar}, and  \eqref{eq:def_G_LR} yields \eqref{eq:ident_F_star_LR_G_LR}.
<!-- end proof-36 -->
<!-- lemma-33: body_md -->
\label{lem:f_TAP_q_star_alpha=0}
    For any $\beta>0,\ \alpha \in (0,1)$ and $\tau_L,\tau_R>0$ s.t. $\tau_L+\tau_R\leqslant1$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:def_ftap_explicit}
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta, \tau_L, \tau_R, \alpha)
        =
        -\frac{1}{2}
        -
        \frac{1}{2} \log (2\beta)        
        +
        G_L(\beta,\tau_L,\alpha)
        +
        \beta I(1-\alpha) 
        (1 - \tau_L - \tau_R)
        +
        G_R(\beta,\tau_R,\alpha).
    \end{equation}
         
                                             
                                                     
           
           
                     
           
                                   
           
                                    
           
                                     
                    
         
    Also for $\alpha=0$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:f_TAP_q_star_alpha=0}
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta, 1-q^\ast, 0, 0)
        =
        -
        \frac{1}{2}
        -
        \frac{1}{2} \log (2\beta)
        +
        G_L(\beta,1-q^\ast,0)
        +
        \beta q^\ast \mu_{\rm max}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-33 -->
<!-- proof-37: body_md -->
These follow by combining \Cref{lem:ftap_explicit_pre} and \Cref{lem:ident_F_star_LR_G_LR}.
<!-- end proof-37 -->
<!-- lemma-34: body_md -->
\label{lem:F_TAP_max_tau}
    For any $\beta>0,\alpha\in(0,1)$ the following holds.
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item
            If $\tau_R>0$:
            \begin{equation}\label{eq:F_TAP_max_tau_L}
                \sup_{\tau_L\in[0,1-q^*]} M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,\tau_L,\tau_R,\alpha)
                =
                M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,1-q^\ast,\tau_R,\alpha).
            \end{equation}
        \item
            for any $\tau_L>0$:
            \begin{equation}\label{eq:F_TAP_max_tau_R}
                \sup_{\tau_R\in [0,q^*]} M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,\tau_L,\tau_R,\alpha)
                =
                M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,\tau_L,q^*,\alpha).
            \end{equation}
        \item           
            \begin{equation}\label{eq:F_TAP_max_tau_LR}
                \sup_{\tau_L\in[0,1-q^*],\tau_R\in[0,q^*]}
                M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,\tau_L,\tau_R,\alpha)
                =
                M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,1-q^*,q^*,\alpha).
            \end{equation}            
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-34 -->
<!-- proof-38: body_md -->
The terms in \eqref{eq:def_ftap_explicit} that depend on $\tau_L$ are $G_L(\beta,\tau_L,\alpha)-\beta I(1-\alpha)\tau_L$, and this expression equals    \begin{equation}\label{eq:F_TAP_max_tau_step_1}    
        \inf_{\eta\geqslant I(1-\alpha)}
        \left\{
            \beta\tau_L(\eta-I(1-\alpha))
            -
            \frac{1}{2}\int_{\mu_{\rm min}}^{I(1-\alpha)}
            \log(\eta-x)\mu(dx)
        \right\}.
    \end{equation}    
    The term $\beta\tau_L(\eta-I(1-\alpha))$ is non-decreasing in $\tau_L$ for all $\eta$ in the range of the infmimum, so \eqref{eq:F_TAP_max_tau_step_1} is clearly non-decreasing in $\tau$. This proves \eqref{eq:F_TAP_max_tau_L}.
    
    Similarly, the terms in \eqref{eq:def_ftap_explicit} that depend on $\tau_R$ are $ G_R(\beta,\tau_R,\alpha)-\beta I(1-\alpha)\tau_R$, and this expression is non-decreasing in $\tau_R$, proving \eqref{eq:F_TAP_max_tau_R}.

    Finally \eqref{eq:F_TAP_max_tau_LR} follows from \eqref{eq:F_TAP_max_tau_L} and \eqref{eq:F_TAP_max_tau_R}.
<!-- end proof-38 -->
<!-- lemma-35: body_md -->
If $\beta>\beta_c$, then
    \begin{equation}
        G_L(\beta,1-q^\ast,0)
        =
        \beta (1-q^\ast) \mu_{\rm max}
        -
        \frac{1}{2}
        \int_{\mu_{\rm min}}^{\mu_{\rm max}}
        \log(\mu_{\rm max}-y)
        \mu(dy).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-35 -->
<!-- proof-39: body_md -->
If $\beta>\beta_c$, then $q^\ast=q^\ast(\beta)>0$, and the function
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:eta_to}
        \eta
        \mapsto
        \beta(1-q^\ast)\eta
        -
        \dfrac{1}{2}
        \int_{\mu_{\rm min}}^{\mu_{\rm max}}
        \log(\eta-y)\mu(dy)
    \end{equation}
    has derivative
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:eta_to_deriv}
        \beta (1-q^\ast)
        -
        \dfrac{1}{2}
        \int_{\mu_{\rm min}}^{\mu_{\rm max}} \dfrac{\mu(dy)}{\eta-y}
        \geqslant
        \beta(1-q^\ast)-\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mu_{\rm min}}^{\mu_{\rm max}} \dfrac{\mu(dy)}{\mu_{\rm max}-y}
        \overset{\eqref{eq:def_smu}}{=}
        \beta(1-q^\ast)
        -
        \dfrac{1}{2} s_{\mu}.
    \end{equation}
    By the definitions \eqref{eq:def_beta_c}, \eqref{eq:def_q_star}, the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:eta_to_deriv} is zero. Thus \eqref{eq:eta_to} is non-decreasing.
<!-- end proof-39 -->
<!-- lemma-36: body_md -->
\label{lem:GL_plus_GR_alpha_ineq}
    For all $\beta>0$ and $\alpha\in(0,1)$
