    \begin{equation}\label{eq:GL_plus_GR_alpha_ineq}
        G_L(\beta,1-q^\ast,\alpha)
        +
        G_R(\beta,q^\ast,\alpha)
        \leqslant
        G_L(\beta,1-q^\ast,0) + \beta q^\ast \mu_{\rm max}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-36 -->
<!-- proof-40: body_md -->
Since the sum of infima is not greater than the infimum of the sum
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:lhs_bound}
        \begin{aligned}
            & G_L(\beta,1-q^\ast,\alpha)
            +
            G_R(\beta,q^\ast,\alpha)
            \\
            &\leqslant
            \inf_{\eta\geqslant\mu_{\rm max}} \left\{\beta(1-q^\ast)\eta-\frac{1}{2}\int^{I(1-\alpha)}_{\mu_{\rm min}}\log(\eta-x)\mu(dx)\right\}+
            \inf_{\eta\geqslant\mu_{\rm max}}\left\{\beta q^\ast\eta-\frac{1}{2}\int^{\mu_{\rm max}}_{I(1-\alpha)}\log(\eta-x)\mu(dx)\right\}
            \\
            &\leqslant\inf_{\eta\geqslant\mu_{\rm max}} \left\{\beta\eta-\dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mu_{\rm min}}^{\mu_{\rm max}} \log(\eta-x)\mu(dx)\right\}
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    If $q^\ast=0$, then the bottom line equals  the r.h.s. of \eqref{eq:GL_plus_GR_alpha_ineq}. If $q^\ast>0$, then by considering the case $\eta = \mu_{\rm max}$ we see that the bottom line is bounded above by
    \begin{equation}
        \beta \mu_{\rm max}
        -
        \dfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mu_{\rm min}}^{\mu_{\rm max}}
        \log(\mu_{\rm max}-x)\mu(dx).
    \end{equation}
    which by \eqref{eq:eta_to} equals the r.h.s. of \eqref{eq:GL_plus_GR_alpha_ineq}. Thus in either case \eqref{eq:GL_plus_GR_alpha_ineq} holds.
<!-- end proof-40 -->
<!-- lemma-37: body_md -->
\label{lem:f_TAP_q_star_sup_in_alpha}
    For any $\beta>0$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:f_TAP_q_star_sup_in_alpha}
        \sup_{\alpha\in(0,1)} M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,1-q^*,q^*,\alpha)
        \leqslant
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,1-q^*,0,0).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-37 -->
<!-- proof-41: body_md -->
This follows from Lemma \ref{lem:f_TAP_q_star_alpha=0} and \ref{lem:GL_plus_GR_alpha_ineq}.
<!-- end proof-41 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:f_TAP_alpha_ineq}
    For all $\beta>0$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:f_TAP_alpha_ineq}
        \sup_{\alpha, \tau_L,\tau_R\in \mathfrak{S}_{\beta}}
        M_\mathrm{TAP} (\beta,\tau_L,\tau_R,\alpha)
        =
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,1-q^\ast,0,0).
          
                                  
    \end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-42: body_md -->
The upper bound follows from statement (3) of Lemma \ref{lem:F_TAP_max_tau} and Lemma \ref{lem:f_TAP_q_star_sup_in_alpha}, while the lower bound simply follows from evaluating $M_\mathrm{TAP}$ at $(\beta,1-q^*,0,0)$.
<!-- end proof-42 -->
<!-- lemma-38: body_md -->
\label{lem:MTAP_fTAP}
    For all $\beta>0$
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:MTAP_fTAP}
        M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,1-q^\ast,0,0) = f_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,\mu_{\rm max} q^\ast,q^\ast)
        =
        \sup_{
        q \in [0,1)
        :
        \;
        \beta(1-q)\leqslant\beta_c
        }
        f_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,q \mu_{\rm max},q).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-38 -->
<!-- proof-43: body_md -->
The first equality follows from \eqref{eq:def_ftap_alpha=0} and \eqref{eq:ftapDefinition} while the second follows from

    \begin{equation}
        \frac{\partial  f_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,q \mu_{\rm max},q)}{\partial q}
        =\beta\big(\mu_{\rm max}-x^*(\beta(1-q))\big)\leqslant 0,
    \end{equation}
    where we used \eqref{eq:ftapDefinition}, Lemma \ref{lem:legendre_diff} and $x^*\geqslant \mu_{\rm max}$.
<!-- end proof-43 -->
<!-- proof-44: body_md -->
Fix some $B>0$. The upper bound
    
    \begin{equation}
        F_N(\beta)
        \leqslant
        \sup_{
        q \in [0,1)
        :
        \;
        \beta(1-q)\leqslant\beta_c
        }
        f_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,q \mu_{\rm max},q)
        +o(1),
    \end{equation}
    which holds uniformly for any $\beta\in[0,B]$ follows by combining \Cref{prop:aux}, Corollary \ref{cor:f_TAP_alpha_ineq} and Lemma \ref{lem:MTAP_fTAP} for $\beta>\beta_c$. For $\beta\leqslant \beta_c$, note that $q=0$ is a valid choice in the above supremum and that $f_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,0,0)=F_{\mu}(\beta)$, using Theorem \ref{thm:sec2_main} concludes the proof of the upper bound. For the lower bound, it also follows from Theorem \ref{thm:sec2_main} for $\beta\leqslant \beta_c$. For $\beta>\beta_c$ simply note that
    \begin{equation}
        F_N(\beta)
        \geqslant 
        \frac{1}{N}
        \log
        Z_N (\beta,A_{0,\epsilon}^{1-q^*,0}).
    \end{equation}
    Moreover, Lemma \ref{lem:Z_Nslice_decompo} proves

    \begin{equation}
    \begin{aligned}
        \frac{1}{N}
        \log Z_N (\beta,A_{0,\epsilon}^{1-q^*,0})
        \geqslant &
        \beta \lambda_N q^*
        +
        \frac{N-1}{N}
        F_{\hat{\mu}_{L,0}}\left(\frac{N}{N-1} \beta (1-q^*)\right) \\
        &+\frac{1}{N}\log E \big[ A_{0,\epsilon}^{1-q^*,0} \big]
        -
        6\beta \Lambda\epsilon.
    \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    A straightforward adaptation of Lemma \ref{lem:measure_AK_K=0_N} proves that 

    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}\log E \left[ A_{0,\epsilon}^{1-q^*,0} \right]
        \geqslant
        - 
        \frac{1}{2}D(0||1-q^*,0)
        -
        c\frac{\log N \epsilon^{-1}}{N}
        -
        \frac{\epsilon}{1-q^*},
    \end{equation}
    while using Lipschitzness and boundedness of $F_{\hat{\mu}_{L,0}}$ yields
    

    \begin{equation}
    \begin{aligned}
        \frac{1}{N}
        \log Z_N (\beta,A_{0,\epsilon}^{1-q^*,0})
        \geqslant &
        \beta \lambda_N q^*
        +
        F_{\hat{\mu}_{L,0}}\left( \beta (1-q^*)\right)
        - 
        \frac{1}{2}D(0||1-q^*,0)\\
        &-
        c\frac{\log N \epsilon^{-1}}{N}
        -
        \frac{\epsilon}{1-q^*}
        -2\frac{\Lambda \beta(1-q^*)}{N}
        -6\beta\Lambda\epsilon.
    \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    Choosing $\epsilon=N^{-1}$ and lower bounding $F_{\hat{\mu}_{L,0}}$ with Theorem \ref{thm:sec2_main} concludes the proof of the Theorem for pointwise convergence (the sequence $\hat{\mu}_{L,0}$ satisfies Assumption \ref{main_assumption}). Because both $F_N$ and $F_\mu$ are $\Lambda$-Lipschitz in $\beta$, a finite-net argument upgrades pointwise convergence to uniform convergence on every compact temperature interval.
<!-- end proof-44 -->
<!-- lemma-39: body_md -->
\label{lem:TAP_on_subspace}
    Let $\mathcal{E}$ be any affine subspace of  $\ {\mathbb{R}}^N$ with $d(0,\mathcal{E})=\alpha$, $\alpha<1$ and fix some $0<\epsilon<1-\alpha$. Let $\overline{\mathcal{E}}=\{x\in {\mathbb{R}}^n, \ d(x,\mathcal{E})\leqslant \epsilon\}$ be its thickened version. Then

    \begin{equation}
        \left|
        F^f_N (\beta,\overline{\mathcal{E}})
        -
        \frac{1}{N}\log \left(E\left[ 1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{E}}} \right]E_{\mathcal{E}}\left[ \exp\left(\beta H^f_N(\sigma)\right) \right]\right)
        \right|
        \leqslant
        \frac{2\beta(\Lambda+\gamma)\epsilon}{\sqrt{1-(\alpha+\epsilon)^2}}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-39 -->
<!-- proof-45: body_md -->
Let us have $P$ the projection of $S^{N-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{E}}$ onto $S^{N-1}\cap \mathcal{E}$. Because on $S_n$ the normalized Hamiltonian $H_N/N$ is $2(\Lambda+\gamma)$ Lipchitz and that for $\sigma \in S^{N-1}\cap\overline{\mathcal{E}}$ we have $|P(\sigma)-\sigma|\leqslant \epsilon/\sqrt{1-(\alpha+\epsilon)^2}$

    \begin{equation}
        \left|
        \frac{1}{N}\log E\left[ \exp\left(\beta H^f_N(\sigma)\right)1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{E}}} \right]
        -
        \frac{1}{N}\log E\left[ \exp\left(\beta H^f_N(P(\sigma))\right)1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{E}}} \right] \right|
        \leqslant
         \frac{2\beta(\Lambda+\gamma)\epsilon}{\sqrt{1-(\alpha+\epsilon)^2}}.
    \end{equation}
    Finally, remark that for $\sigma$ uniformly distributed on $S^{N-1} \cap \overline{\mathcal{E}}$, $P(\sigma)$ is uniformly distributed on $ S^{N-1} \cap \mathcal{E}$, concluding the proof of the lemma.
<!-- end proof-45 -->
<!-- lemma-40: body_md -->
\label{lem:measure_small_sphere}
Let $N\geqslant2$, $\mathcal{E}\subset\mathbb R^N$ be an affine subspace
with $\alpha=d(0,\mathcal{E})<1$, and let $\overline{\mathcal{E}}=\{x\in\mathbb R^N:d(x,\mathcal{E})\leqslant\epsilon\}$. Then
\begin{equation}
    \frac{1}{N}\log \left(E\left[ 1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{E}}} \right]\right)
    \leqslant
    \frac12\log(1-\alpha^2)
    +
    \frac{\alpha\epsilon}{1-\alpha^2}
    -
    \frac{\log(1-\alpha^2)}{2N}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-40 -->
<!-- proof-46: body_md -->
If $\alpha=0$ the result is trivial, so suppose $\alpha>0$. Let $m$ be the smallest norm point of $\mathcal{E}$ and set $e=m/|m|$. Since $m$ is orthogonal to $\mathcal{E}-m$, every $x\in \mathcal{E}$ satisfies $\langle x,e\rangle=\alpha$. Thus
\begin{equation}
    \overline{\mathcal{E}}\cap S^{N-1}
    \subset
    \{\sigma\in S^{N-1}; \ \langle\sigma,e\rangle\geqslant\alpha-\epsilon\}.
\end{equation}
By rotational invariance, write $\sigma=G/|G|$,
where $G=(G_1,\ldots,G_N)$ are independent standard Gaussian
variables, and take $e=e_1$. Setting
$S=\sum_{i=2}^N G_i^2$, the Gaussian tail bound gives
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\mathbb P(\langle\sigma,e\rangle\geqslant t)
&=
\mathbb P\left(
G_1\geqslant\frac{t}{\sqrt{1-t^2}}\sqrt S
\right)\\
&\leqslant
\mathbb E\exp\left(-\frac{t^2S}{2(1-t^2)}\right)
=(1-t^2)^{(N-1)/2}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
If $\epsilon<\alpha$, applying the above at $t=\alpha-\epsilon\in(0,1)$ proves 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:volume_slice_first_claim}
    \frac1N\log \left(E\left[ 1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{E}}} \right]\right)
    \leqslant
    \frac{N-1}{2N}
    \log\bigl(1-(\alpha-\epsilon)_+^2\bigr),
\end{equation}
and the above is also trivially true if $\epsilon\geqslant\alpha$. Then, set $s=1-\alpha^2$ and
$b=1-(\alpha-\epsilon)_+^2$. Then $s\leqslant b\leqslant s+2\alpha\epsilon$. Hence
\begin{equation}
    \begin{aligned}
    \frac{N-1}{2N}\log b
    &=
    \frac12\log s+\frac12\log(b/s)-\frac1{2N}\log b\\
    &\leqslant
    \frac12\log s+\frac{\alpha\epsilon}{s}
    +\frac{|\log s|}{2N},
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
which concludes the proof of the lemma.
<!-- end proof-46 -->
<!-- lemma-41: body_md -->
\label{lem:measure_sphere_LB}
    Let $N\geqslant k+2$, $\mathcal{E}\subset {\mathbb{R}}^N$ be an affine subspace of dimension $N-k$ with $\alpha=d(0,\mathcal{E})<1$ and, for $0<\epsilon < (1-\alpha^2)/6$, let $\overline{\mathcal{E}}=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^N \ : \ d(x,\mathcal{E})\leqslant \epsilon\}$. Then

    \begin{equation}
    \frac{1}{N}\log \left(E\left[ 1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{E}}} \right]\right)
    \geqslant
    \frac12\log(1-\alpha^2)
    -
    \frac{3\epsilon}{1-\alpha^2}
    +
    \frac{k}{N}\log \epsilon.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-41 -->
<!-- proof-47: body_md -->
The statement is trivial if $k=0$, so suppose $k>0$. Letting $m$ be the smallest norm element of $\mathcal{E}$ we write $\mathcal{E}=m+V$, where $V$ is a linear subspace of dimension $N-k$ and $m\perp V$. Assume $1\leqslant k\leqslant N-2$ and $0<\epsilon\leqslant (1-|m|^2)/6$. Since $d(\sigma,\mathcal E)=|P^{V^\perp}\sigma-m|$ we want to lower bound the probability of the ball $B=\{ y\in V^\perp; \ d(m,y)\leqslant \epsilon \}$ when $y=P^{V^\perp}\sigma$. Let $\ell_k$ denote Lebesgue measure on $V^\perp$, normalized so that $\ell_k(B(0,1))=1$. The beta-distribution identity
in \cite[Section~2, p.~403]{diaconis1987dozen} gives that $y$ has density, with respect to $\ell_k$,

\begin{equation}
    p(y)
    =
    \frac{\Gamma(N/2)}
    {\Gamma((N-k)/2)\Gamma(k/2+1)}
    (1-|y|^2)^{(N-k-2)/2}\mathbf1_{\{|y|<1\}}.
\end{equation}
Moreover, $1-|y|^2\geqslant 1-|m|^2-3\epsilon$ whenever $y\in B$ and this ball has volume $\epsilon^k$ with respect to $\ell_k$. Hence

\begin{equation}
    E[\mathbf1_{\overline{\mathcal E}}]
    \geqslant
    \frac{\Gamma(N/2)}
    {\Gamma((N-k)/2)\Gamma(k/2+1)}
    \epsilon^k\big(1-|m|^2-3\epsilon\big)^{(N-k-2)/2}
    \geqslant
    \epsilon^k \big(1-|m|^2-3\epsilon\big)^{N/2}.
\end{equation}
Therefore

\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\frac1N\log E[\mathbf1_{\overline{\mathcal E}}]
&\geqslant
\frac12\log(1-|m|^2-3\epsilon)
+
\frac{k}{N}\log \epsilon\\
&\geqslant
\frac12\log(1-|m|^2)
-\frac{3\epsilon}{1-|m|^2}
+
\frac{k}{N}\log \epsilon.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Replacing $|m|$ by $\alpha$ concludes the proof of the lemma.
<!-- end proof-47 -->
<!-- lemma-42: body_md -->
\label{lem:sec7.1_main}
    Let $\mathcal{E}$ be any affine subspace of dimension $n$ intersecting $S^{N-1}$, of uniformly bounded codimension and chosen so that the effective external field on $\mathcal{E}$ is null. Let $m$ be its smallest norm element. Then

    \begin{equation}
    \begin{aligned}
        \frac{1}{N}\log E_{\mathcal{E}} \left[ \exp\left(\beta H_N(\sigma)\right)  \right]
        \leqslant \ &
                                                                                                                                                                                                
                 
        \sup_{\alpha,\tau_L,\tau_R\in S_{\mathcal{E}}}F_{\mathrm{TAP}}\big(\beta,m,\tau_L,\tau_R,\alpha\big)\\
        &-
        \frac{1}{2}\log\left( 1-|m|^2 \right)
        +
        2\frac{N-n}{n}\beta\Lambda
        +
        \mathrm{Err}_n \big(\beta,\mu_N^{\mathcal{E}}\big),
    \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    where $\mu_N^{\mathcal{E}}$ is the empirical distribution stemming from the eigenvalues of $M_N$ restricted to the linear subspace $\mathcal{E}-m$, $\mathrm{Err}_N$ is as in Proposition \ref{prop:aux} and the supremum is taken over the set

    \begin{equation}\label{eq:def_sup_set}
        S_{\mathcal{E}}
        :=
        [0,1] \times [0,1-q^{*,\mathcal{E}}]
        \times
        [0,q^{*,\mathcal{E}}].
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-42 -->
<!-- proof-48: body_md -->
Because $S^{N-1} \cap \mathcal{E}$ is also a sphere, but of smaller dimension and radius, the natural first step is to remark that for $\sigma$ uniform on $S^{N-1}\cap \mathcal{E}$, $\sigma-m$ is uniform on a sphere of radius $\sqrt{1-|m|^2}$. Hence using the
    decomposition of \eqref{eq:hamiltonian_decompo}, valid on affine subspace where effective external field vanishes, and the homogeneity of the Hamiltonian
    
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{N}\log E_{\mathcal{E}}\left[ \exp \beta H_N(\sigma) \right]
        =
        \frac{1}{N}\beta H_N(m)
        +
        \frac{1}{N}\log E_{V}\left[ \exp \beta (1-|m|^2) H_N(\sigma) \right].
    \end{equation}
    We start by taking into account that the dimension of $V=\mathcal{E}-m$ is $n$ rather than $N$. We get

    \begin{equation}
    \begin{aligned}
        \frac{1}{N}\log E_{V}\left[ \exp \beta (1-|m|^2) H_N(\sigma) \right]
        = &
        \frac{n}{N}\frac{1}{n}\log E_{V}\left[ \exp \tfrac{N}{n}\beta (1-|m|^2) H_n(\sigma) \right]\\
        \leqslant &
        \frac{1}{n}\log E_{V}\left[ \exp\beta (1-|m|^2) H_n(\sigma) \right]
        +
        2\frac{N-n}{n}\beta\Lambda,
    \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    where we used Lipschitzness and the abuse of notation $H_n(\sigma)=\frac{n}{N}H_N(\sigma)=n\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{Dim}(\mathcal{E})}\lambda_i^{\mathcal{E}}\sigma_i^2$. Then, the reasoning is as in Section \ref{sec:intermediate_TAP} but at temperature $\beta (1-|m|^2)$ and dimension $n$. We start by checking that $\mu_N^{\mathcal{E}}$ satisfies Assumption \ref{main_assumption}. Poincaré separation Theorem (also known as the Cauchy interlacing theorem), states that for $i\in [\mathrm{Dim}(\mathcal{E})]$ 
    
    \begin{equation}
        \lambda_i \leqslant
        \lambda_i^{ \mathcal{E}}
        \leqslant
        \lambda_{i+N-n}.
    \end{equation}
    As a direct consequence, if $\mathrm{Dim}(\mathcal{E}^\perp)$ is bounded then $\mu_N^{\mathcal{E}}$ converges to $\mu$ and satisfies all the assumptions of Assumption \ref{main_assumption}. Start by supposing $\beta(1-|m|^2)>\beta_c$ and fix some $\delta>0$. Recall the partition 

    \begin{equation}
        S^{N-1}\cap \mathcal{E}
        \subset
        \bigcup_{K,\tau_L,\tau_R\in \widehat{S}_{\mathcal{E}}}
        A_{K,\tau_L, \tau_R,\delta}^{\mathcal{E}}
    \end{equation}
    where $\widehat{S}_{\mathcal{E}}$ is a discretized version of $S_{\mathcal{E}}$

    \begin{equation}\label{eq:def_sup_set_discrete}
        \widehat{S}_{\mathcal{E}}
        :=
        [0,n-1] \times [0,2\delta,\dots,1-q^{*,\mathcal{E}}]
        \times
        [0,2\delta, \dots,q^{*,\mathcal{E}}].
    \end{equation}
    Proposition \ref{prop:aux} with an appropriate choice of $\delta>0$ gives

    \begin{equation}    
    \begin{aligned}
        \tfrac{1}{n}\log E_{V}\left[ \exp \left( \beta (1-|m|^2) H_n(\sigma)\right)  \right]
        \leqslant \ &
        \sup_{\alpha,\tau_L,\tau_R\in S_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathrm{On}(\beta(1-|m|^2),\tau_L,\tau_R,\alpha)\\
        &+
        \mathrm{Err}_n \big(\beta,\mu_N^{\mathcal{E}}\big),
    \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    where, as noted earlier, the Onsager term is $M_\mathrm{TAP}(\beta,\tau_L,\tau_R,\alpha)$ and is defined in \eqref{eq:def_ftap}. On the other hand, if $\beta(1-|m|^2)\leqslant\beta_c$, Theorem \ref{thm:sec2_main} gives the required bound since 
    
    \begin{equation}
        F_\mu\big(\beta(1-|m|^2)\big)=
        \mathrm{On}\big(\beta(1-|m|^2),1,0,0\big).
    \end{equation}
    and $(0,1,0)\in\mathcal S_{\mathcal{E}}$. Recalling the definition of $F_{\mathrm{TAP}}$ \eqref{eq:final_def_Ftap} concludes the proof of the Lemma.
<!-- end proof-48 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor:external_field_main}
    Let $\mathcal{E}$ be any affine subspace of dimension at least $N-k$ intersecting $S^{N-1}$, chosen so that the effective external field on $\mathcal{E}$ is null. Let $m$ be the center of $\mathcal{E}$ and $\overline{\mathcal{E}}=\left\{x\in \mathbb{R}^N, \ d(x,\mathcal{E})\leqslant \epsilon \right\}$ be its thickened version. Then, for any $0<\epsilon<1-|m|$

    \begin{equation}
    \begin{aligned}
        F_N(\beta,\overline{\mathcal{E}})
        \leqslant \ &
        \sup_{\alpha,\tau_L,\tau_R \in S_{\mathcal{E}}}F_{\rm TAP}\big(\beta,m,\tau_L,\tau_R,\alpha\big)
        +
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(7)} \big(\beta,\mu_N^{\mathcal{E}},|m|,\epsilon\big),
    \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    where the error term is given by

    \begin{equation}
    \begin{aligned}
        \mathrm{Err}_N^{(7)} \big(\beta,\mu_N^{\mathcal{E}},|m|,\epsilon\big)
        = &
        \frac{2\beta(\Lambda+\gamma)\epsilon}{\sqrt{1-(|m|+\epsilon)^2}}
        +
        \frac{|m|\epsilon}{1-|m|^2}
        -
        \frac{\log(1-|m|^2)}{2N}\\
        &+
        2\frac{k}{N-k}\beta\Lambda
        +
        \mathrm{Err}_{N-k} \big(\beta,\mu_N^{\mathcal{E}}\big)
    \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-49: body_md -->
The proof is immediate combining the results of  Lemma \ref{lem:TAP_on_subspace}, \ref{lem:measure_small_sphere} and \ref{lem:sec7.1_main}.
<!-- end proof-49 -->
<!-- lemma-43: body_md -->
\label{lem:extfield_exclude_small_alpha}
    For $N\geqslant 2$, $\epsilon,\rho$ small enough that $\epsilon,\rho<\exp\big(-8\beta(\Lambda+\gamma)-6\big)$, for any affine subset with center $m$, if $|m|>1-\rho$, then

    \begin{equation}
        F_N^f(\beta,\overline{\mathcal{E}})\leqslant F_N^f(\beta)-1.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-43 -->
<!-- proof-50: body_md -->
Recall that the Hamiltonian is bounded by $-N(\Lambda+\gamma) \leqslant H_N^f\leqslant N( \Lambda+\gamma)$, in particular $F_N^f(\beta)\geqslant -\beta(\Lambda+\gamma)$. In the meantime, Lemma \ref{lem:measure_small_sphere} and \eqref{eq:volume_slice_first_claim} give an upper bound on the measure of $\overline{\mathcal{E}}$, which leads to
    
    
    \begin{equation}
        F_N^f (\beta,\overline{\mathcal{E}})
        \leqslant
        \beta (\Lambda+ \gamma)
        +
        \frac{1}{4}\log\left( 1-(|m|-\epsilon)_+^2 \right)
        \leqslant
        \beta (\Lambda+ \gamma)
        +
        \frac{1}{4}\log(2\rho+2\epsilon).
    \end{equation}
    The proof of the lemma follows.
<!-- end proof-50 -->
<!-- lemma-44: body_md -->
\label{lem:energy_at_norm_q_increases}
Suppose that Assumption \ref{main_assumption} holds. Let $U_N \subseteq \mathbb R^N$
be a subspace of dimension $d_N=O(1)$, and let
$f_N:\overline{B_N}\to\mathbb R$ be continuous and satisfy
\begin{equation}
    f_N(m)=f_N(P^{U_N}m)
    \qquad\text{for all }m\in\overline{B_N}.
\end{equation}
Define the normalized maximal energy at squared norm $q$ by
\begin{equation}
    E_N(q)
    :=\frac1N\max_{|m|^2=q}H_N^f(m)
    =\max_{|m|^2=q}
      \bigl\{m^\top M_Nm+f_N(m)\bigr\},
    \qquad q\in[0,1).
\end{equation}
For every $N\geqslant d_N+3$ and $0\leqslant q\leqslant q'<1$,
\begin{equation}
    E_N(q')
    \geqslant E_N(q)+(q'-q)\lambda_{N-d_N-2}.
\end{equation}
Consequently, for every $q^*\in[0,1)$,

\begin{equation}
    0
    \leqslant
    \sup_{q\in[0,q^*]}
       \bigl\{E_N(q)-q\mu_{\rm max}\bigr\}
       -\bigl\{E_N(q^*)-q^*\mu_{\rm max}\bigr\}
    \leqslant
    q^*\bigl|\lambda_{N-d_N-2}-\mu_{\rm max}\bigr|
    =o(1).
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-44 -->
<!-- proof-51: body_md -->
Fix $N\geqslant d_N+3$ and $0\leqslant q<q'<1$. By continuity and
compactness, there exists $m\in\mathbb R^N$ such that

\begin{equation}
    |m|^2=q,
    \qquad
    E_N(q)=m^\top M_Nm+f_N(m).
\end{equation}
Let $V_N$ be the span of $d_N+3$ orthonormal eigenvectors
corresponding to the largest eigenvalues of $M_N$. Since

\begin{equation}
    \dim\bigl(U_N+\operatorname{span}\{m,M_Nm\}\bigr)
    \leqslant d_N+2,
\end{equation}
there exists a unit vector

\begin{equation}
    v\in V_N\cap
    \bigl(U_N+\operatorname{span}\{m,M_Nm\}\bigr)^\perp.
\end{equation}
In particular,

\begin{equation}
    \langle v,m\rangle=\langle v,M_Nm\rangle=0,
    \qquad P^{U_N}v=0,
    \qquad v^\top M_Nv\geqslant\lambda_{N-d_N-2}.
\end{equation}
Set $m'=m+\sqrt{q'-q}\,v$. Then $|m'|^2=q'$ and $P^{U_N}m'=P^{U_N}m$, so
$f_N(m')=f_N(m)$. Moreover, the cross term vanishes:

\begin{equation}
    (m')^\top M_Nm'
    =m^\top M_Nm+(q'-q)v^\top M_Nv.
\end{equation}
It follows that

\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
    E_N(q')
    &\geqslant (m')^\top M_Nm'+f_N(m')\\
    &\geqslant E_N(q)+(q'-q)\lambda_{N-d_N-2}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The same inequality is immediate when $q=q'$.

Taking $q'=q^*$ and rearranging gives, for every $q\in[0,q^*]$,

\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
    E_N(q)-q\mu_{\rm max}
    &\leqslant E_N(q^*)-q^*\mu_{\rm max}
       +(q^*-q)
         \bigl(\mu_{\rm max}-\lambda_{N-d_N-2}\bigr)\\
    &\leqslant E_N(q^*)-q^*\mu_{\rm max}
       +q^*\bigl|\mu_{\rm max}-\lambda_{N-d_N-2}\bigr|.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Taking the supremum proves the claimed upper bound; the lower
bound follows by choosing $q=q^*$.
Finally, Assumption~1 implies
$\lambda_{N-d_N-2}\to\mu_{\rm max}$, which completes the proof of the lemma.
<!-- end proof-51 -->
<!-- proof-52: body_md -->
A direct consequence of Lemma \ref{lem:energy_at_norm_q_increases} is that the supremum in \eqref{eq:supremum_non_plefka}, up to a vanishing error, is reached at $|m|^2=q^*$. As it is equivalent to $\beta(1-|m|^2)=\beta_c$, this fact alongside \eqref{eq:Good_part_sup_external_field} and \eqref{eq:external_field_main_variational_UB_bis} yields


                                                        
                 
                                                                        
       
                      
       
                                           
           
                                              
       
                                       
               
                
                                                                                                                                                                   

\begin{equation}\label{eq:external_field_final_UB}
    F_N^f(\beta)
    \leqslant
    \sup_{m\in B_N: \ \beta(1-|m|^2)\leqslant \beta_c}  F_{\mathrm{TAP}}(m)
    +o(1).
\end{equation}
To conclude, it suffices to prove the corresponding lower bound. We start by noting that $F_{\mathrm{TAP}}(m)\leqslant \beta(2\Lambda+\gamma)+\frac{1}{2}\log(1-|m|^2)$, so there exists $\rho>0$ small enough that

\begin{equation}\label{eq:sup_m_less-1-rho}
    \sup_{m\in B_N: \ \beta(1-|m|^2)\leqslant \beta_c}  F_{\mathrm{TAP}}(m)
    =
    \sup_{m\in (1-\rho) B_N: \ \beta(1-|m|^2)\leqslant \beta_c}  F_{\mathrm{TAP}}(m).
\end{equation}
Next, remark that for any affine subspace $\mathcal{E}$, $m$ its point of smallest norm and $\overline{\mathcal{E}}$ its thickened version, Lemma \ref{lem:TAP_on_subspace} yields

\begin{equation}
    F_N^f(\beta)
    \geqslant
    F_N^f(\beta,\overline{\mathcal{E}})
    \geqslant 
    \frac1N\log\mathbb E_{\mathcal{E}}e^{\beta H_N^f(\sigma)}
    +
    \frac{1}{N}\log E\left[ 1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{E}}} \right]    
    -
    \frac{2\beta(\Lambda+\gamma)\epsilon}{\sqrt{1-(|m|+\epsilon)^2}}
\end{equation}
We choose $\overline{\mathcal{E}}$ as follows. Fix $m$ satisfying $\beta(1-|m|^2)\leqslant\beta_c$ and $|m|\leqslant1-\rho$. Set $V=\operatorname{span}\{u,m,M_Nm\}^{\perp}$ and $n=\dim V$. On the slice $\mathcal{E}=m+V$, the external field is constant and the effective external field vanishes. Consequently, if $\nu$ denotes the empirical spectral distribution of the projection of $M_N$ to $V$, then

\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
    \frac1N\log E_{\mathcal{E}}e^{\beta H_N^f(\sigma)}
    =&
    \frac{\beta}{N}H_N^f(m)
    +
    \frac{n}{N}\frac1n\log E_{\mathcal{E}-m}\exp\left(\frac{N}{n}\beta\big(1-|m|\big)^2 H_n(\sigma)\right)\\
    \geqslant&
    \frac{\beta}{N}H_N^f(m)
    +\hat{F}_{\nu}\left(\beta (1-|m|^2) \right)
    -
    \frac{6}{N}\beta\Lambda,
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
where we used boundedness and Lipschitzness to get rid of the $N/n$ terms. Since $N-n\leqslant3$, the Cauchy interlacing Theorem and Theorem \ref{thm:sec2_main} imply, uniformly in $m$,

\begin{equation}
    \hat{F}_{\nu}\left(\beta (1-|m|^2)\right)
    =
    F_{\mu}\big(\beta (1-|m|^2)\big)+o(1).
\end{equation}
Moreover, Lemma \ref{lem:measure_sphere_LB} gives

\begin{equation}
    \frac{1}{N}\log E\left[ 1\negmedspace\mathrm{I}_{\overline{\mathcal{E}}} \right]
    \geqslant
    \frac{1}{2}\log\big(1-|m|^2\big)
    -
    \frac{3\epsilon}{\rho}
    +
    \frac{3}{N}\log \epsilon
\end{equation}
Hence, choosing $\epsilon=1/N$ gives, uniformly in $m\in (1-\rho)B_N$,

\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
    F_N^f(\beta)
    \geqslant &
    \frac{\beta}{N}H_N^f(m)
    +\frac12\log\big(1-|m|^2\big)+F_{\mu}\big(\beta (1-|m|^2)\big)
    -o(1)\\
    \geqslant&
    F_{\mathrm{TAP}}(m)
    -o(1).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Taking the supremum over $m$ with $|m|\leqslant 1-\rho$ and recalling \eqref{eq:sup_m_less-1-rho} proves

\begin{equation}\label{eq:external_field_final_LB}
    F_N^f(\beta)
    \geqslant
    \sup_{m\in B_N: \ \beta(1-|m|^2)\leqslant \beta_c}  F_{\mathrm{TAP}}(m)
    -o(1),
\end{equation}
which together with \eqref{eq:external_field_final_UB} concludes the proof of Theorem \ref{thm:main_with_ext_field} for linear external field.

For the general external field, we may subtract $f_N(0)$,
since this shifts both sides of the claimed TAP formula by
$\beta f_N(0)$. Thus, assume $f_N(0)=0$, so that
$|f_N|\leqslant c$ on $B_N$.

In the covering construction, include $U_N$ among the
constrained directions. The resulting slice directions
satisfy $V\subset U_N^\perp$, and hence
$f_N(m+v)=f_N(m)$ on every slice.
On its $\epsilon$-thickening,
\begin{equation}
    |f_N(\sigma)-f_N(m)|\leqslant c\epsilon.
\end{equation}
The preceding estimates therefore apply with the Lipschitz
constant $c$ in place of $|\gamma|$.
<!-- end proof-52 -->
